# Dependency map

Everything Beethoven for VS Code relies on, one entry per assumption: what must be true, how to
check it, what a failure looks like, what repairs it and what could break it later. It comes from
a read-only study of the code on 2026-09-28 (four independent readers and a completeness critic;
raw data in `docs/research/dependency_map_2026-09-28.json`). It is the reference behind the
runtime graph in `docs/DIAGNOSTICS.md`: the troubleshooter checks the entries that can be checked
cheaply and often; this map keeps the rest, so a failure that the troubleshooter cannot name can
still be looked up by its message.

370 entries (395 before merging duplicates). Generated by `tools/depmap_doc.py`.
Claims marked *found by reading* in an entry were not run by the study; `docs/DIAGNOSTICS.md` lists
which of them were later confirmed and fixed.

The study read the code when the product was called Ashley (Ashley for VS Code, up to 3.0.0), and
its entries keep the names of that day, error messages included. Since 4.0.0 (2026-09-30) read
them this way: `~/.ashley` is `~/.beethoven`, `grid/ashley` and `ashleyd.js` are `grid/beethoven`
and `beethovend.js`, `~/.ashley-local` is `~/.beethoven-local` on a new computer, the `ashley.*`
settings and command ids are `beethoven.*`, the "Ashley:" commands are "Beethoven:" in the
Command Palette, the `ashley_*` MCP tools are `beethoven_*`, `ashley-cli` is `beethoven-cli`, and
`/rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley` is `shared/beethoven`. The old paths on disk and on the grid
still work as links, and the old settings and command ids are still read as aliases
(`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "The names from before 4.0.0").

Since 5.0.0 (2026-10-04) there is no API key: the entries about an Anthropic API key, the
`apikey` account, rotation onto it after a limit and `accounts add-key` describe a feature that
was removed (no usage credits, by anyone, ever). A spent subscription window is now answered
`rate_limited` with `retry_after`, and the request is sent again after the reset; a key an
earlier version stored is never used, only marked `disabled` for workers still on grid scripts 7
(`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Credentials").

## Laptop (34)

### `laptop.os-platform` Laptop OS and architecture are supported

- **Assumes:** The laptop is Windows 10 1809+ or Windows 11 (x64/arm64), macOS 12+ (Intel or Apple silicon), or a glibc Linux desktop. process.platform is win32, darwin or linux. Ashley is native to each OS; it neither mirrors nor uses WSL (design rule of 2026-09-27).
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: read process.platform, process.arch and os.release(). Windows passes if [Environment]::OSVersion.Version.Build >= 17763 (PowerShell). macOS passes if `sw_vers -productVersion` >= 12. Linux passes if `ldd --version` prints glibc.
- **Looks like:** `Unable to install extension 'openspineconsortium.ashley-wsu' as it is not compatible with VS Code`; `'ssh' is not recognized as an internal or external command, operable program or batch file.`
- **By hand:** Use a supported laptop (Windows 10 1809+/11, macOS 12+, or a mainstream Linux desktop). Ask the lead for a loaner if needed.
- **Could break when:** Windows 10 went out of support in Oct 2025, and WSU C&IT may block unsupported builds from campus Wi-Fi or the VPN. VS Code and the Claude Code native binary drop old macOS releases over time.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:120-122; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:6-12; extension/package.json:13-18

### `laptop.vscode-install-kind` VS Code is Microsoft's build, installed at a stable path

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** Ashley needs Microsoft's VS Code, installed normally. VSCodium, Cursor and Windsurf lack the Microsoft auth provider and Microsoft's Remote-SSH, and the getExtension ids differ there. The Flatpak build is sandboxed from the host's ssh and ~/.ssh. On macOS the app must run from /Applications, not from App Translocation. On Linux use the .deb/.rpm, not the snap. process.execPath is registered as the MCP runtime, and under the snap or translocation that path changes.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: vscode.env.appName starts with 'Visual Studio Code' and vscode.env.uriScheme is 'vscode' or 'vscode-insiders'. process.execPath contains none of '/snap/', '/AppTranslocation/' or a Flatpak prefix, and process.env.FLATPAK_ID is undefined.
- **Looks like:** `No authentication provider 'microsoft' is currently registered.`; `getExtension('ms-vscode-remote.remote-ssh') undefined in a VS Code fork`; `MCP server ashley: Failed to connect (after a snap refresh or macOS relaunch)`; `spawn ssh ENOENT (Flatpak)`
- **By hand:** Install VS Code from code.visualstudio.com. On macOS drag it into /Applications. On Linux use the .deb/.rpm package.
- **Could break when:** More students use AI-IDE forks. Microsoft may change appName or uriScheme, and snap or Flatpak packaging may change.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:92-95,166-173; extension/src/vscode/mcpRegister.ts:19-32; extension/src/extension.ts:200-203

### `laptop.home-directory` The home directory Ashley writes is the one ssh reads

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** os.homedir() is local and writable, and it is the directory whose .ssh the ssh client reads: %USERPROFILE% on Windows, $HOME elsewhere. expandHome() expands only a leading '~/', so ashley.identityFile must use tilde form or an absolute path ('~\' is not expanded).
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -G wsu-grid` has an identityfile line equal to <os.homedir()>/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu (normalise separators). In the extension host, fs.accessSync(os.homedir(), fs.constants.W_OK) succeeds. On Windows, %HOME% is unset or equal to %USERPROFILE%.
- **Looks like:** `no such identity: C:\Users\<user>/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu: No such file or directory`; `EPERM: operation not permitted, mkdir`; `key created under C:\Users\<user>\.ssh while Git's ssh reads H:\.ssh (HOME redirected to a network share)`
- **By hand:** Remove a user-level HOME variable, or point it at %USERPROFILE%. Alternatively, set ashley.identityFile to an absolute path.
- **Could break when:** University-managed Windows images with folder redirection or roaming profiles.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:115-118; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:139-141; extension/package.json:288-292

### `laptop.openssh` OpenSSH client installed (>= 7.6)

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** An OpenSSH client >= 7.6 is installed. It must support StrictHostKeyChecking accept-new, the '+' algorithm syntax, ProxyJump and `ssh -G`. On Windows that is the 'OpenSSH Client' optional feature in System32\OpenSSH (9.5p1 measured on Windows 11). macOS uses /usr/bin/ssh; Linux uses the openssh-client package.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -V` (prints to stderr) matches /OpenSSH[^_]*_([0-9]+)\.([0-9]+)/ with version >= 7.6. On Windows also `Get-WindowsCapability -Online -Name OpenSSH.Client*` reports State Installed.
- **Looks like:** `'ssh' is not recognized as an internal or external command`; `spawn ssh ENOENT (run() returns code 127)`; `Bad SSH2 Mac spec '+hmac-sha1'`; `command-line line 0: unsupported option "accept-new".`
- **Repair:** Only with the user's consent and a UAC prompt. Windows: `Add-WindowsCapability -Online -Name OpenSSH.Client~~~~0.0.1.0`. Linux: `pkexec apt-get install -y openssh-client` (or dnf). macOS: nothing to install.
- **By hand:** Windows: Settings > System > Optional features > Add a feature > OpenSSH Client, then restart VS Code.
- **Could break when:** Windows moving to OpenSSH 10.x, whose new default KEX (mlkem768x25519) is untested against the grid's 7.4. Microsoft could ship OpenSSH from another path (winget, Program Files). A future client could drop hmac-sha1, which the grid has required from Windows.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:94-113; extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:17-30; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:241

### `laptop.openssh-on-path` spawn('ssh') resolves to the working OpenSSH

Depends on: `laptop.openssh`, `laptop.vscode-install-kind`

- **Assumes:** run('ssh'/'scp'/'ssh-keygen') in the extension host, the MCP server and the CLI resolves on PATH to the same working OpenSSH that Remote-SSH uses. On Windows, libuv tries only .exe/.com on PATH. resolveExecutable(), which prefers System32\OpenSSH, is applied only to terminals, not to run(), relay or scp.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: run('ssh', ['-V']) exits 0 with 'OpenSSH' in stderr. On Windows the first line of `where.exe ssh` is C:\Windows\System32\OpenSSH\ssh.exe or equals remote.SSH.path; on macOS/Linux use `command -v ssh`. Compare with resolveExecutable('ssh').
- **Looks like:** `spawn ssh ENOENT`; `run() code 127`; `C:\Program Files\Git\usr\bin\ssh.exe first on PATH (version string lacks 'for_Windows')`; `Unable to resolve your shell environment in a reasonable time (macOS VS Code)`; `terminal login works but the tree says 'Cannot reach the grid'`
- **Repair:** Before spawning, prepend %SystemRoot%\System32\OpenSSH to process.env.PATH in the extension host (safe, process-local). Code fix: route run(), scp and the relay through resolveExecutable().
- **By hand:** Put C:\Windows\System32\OpenSSH before Git's usr\bin in PATH, or set remote.SSH.path to the same ssh.exe.
- **Could break when:** Git for Windows updating its bundled ssh. Windows moving OpenSSH out of System32. VS Code changing how it resolves the shell environment on macOS.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:30-44,103-113; extension/src/vscode/ui.ts:85-98; extension/src/core/relay.ts:40; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:148,287; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:298

### `laptop.ssh-keygen` ssh-keygen creates an ed25519 key

Depends on: `laptop.openssh`

- **Assumes:** `ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -N '' -C '<id>@wayne.edu ashley' -f <key>` works through spawn with an empty-string argument. On Windows it sets an owner-only ACL on the private key.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -N "" -q -f <scratch>/ashley_probe` exits 0 and <scratch>/ashley_probe.pub exists; delete both afterwards. Once the key exists: `ssh-keygen -l -f ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu.pub` prints '(ED25519)'.
- **Looks like:** `ssh-keygen failed: ...`; `Saving key "..." failed: No such file or directory`; `unknown key type ed25519`
- **Repair:** Run stepSshKey.run: it creates ~/.ssh and generates the key, and never overwrites an existing pair.
- **By hand:** Run the wizard step 'SSH key' again, or generate the key by hand with the command above.
- **Could break when:** A FIPS-mode managed laptop may refuse ed25519, which would need an ecdsa fallback.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:274-292

### `laptop.scp` scp uploads the grid scripts into an existing ~/.ashley/bin

Depends on: `laptop.openssh-on-path`, `account.grid-key-authorized`

- **Assumes:** `scp -q <batch opts> <files...> wsu-grid:~/.ashley/bin/` copies the six grid files, and the target directory already exists. OpenSSH >= 9.0 clients use the SFTP protocol by default, which needs the grid's sftp-server subsystem; the 7.4 server lacks the expand-path extension. The upload has worked from Greg's laptop, where the directory existed. How the 9.x client treats '~/' against this server when the directory is missing is not recorded.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'test -d ~/.ashley/bin && echo ok'` prints ok. Then `scp -q -o BatchMode=yes <1-byte file> wsu-grid:~/.ashley/bin/.ashley_probe` exits 0, followed by `ssh wsu-grid 'rm -f ~/.ashley/bin/.ashley_probe'`.
- **Looks like:** `upload failed: scp: ...: No such file or directory`; `subsystem request failed on channel 0`; `scp: Connection closed`
- **Repair:** Create the directory with a shell-expanded command, `ssh wsu-grid 'mkdir -p ~/.ashley/bin ~/.ashley/log'`, and retry. If the SFTP subsystem is refused, retry with `scp -O` (legacy protocol).
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Install or update Ashley on the grid' again after the directory exists. Send the log to the lead.
- **Could break when:** C&IT disabling the SFTP subsystem. OpenSSH removing legacy scp (-O). A newer grid sshd adding expand-path (benign).
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:110-113; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:527-535

### `laptop.ssh-dir-permissions` ~/.ssh and ~/.ssh/config are owner-only

Depends on: `laptop.home-directory`

- **Assumes:** ~/.ssh (700) and ~/.ssh/config (600) belong to the user and are not writable by others. Ashley writes config with mode 0o600 but never chmods an existing .ssh. On Windows Node ignores the mode, and OpenSSH enforces NTFS ACLs (only the user, SYSTEM and Administrators).
- **Check:** Laptop, macOS/Linux: `stat -c %a ~/.ssh ~/.ssh/config` (macOS: `stat -f %Lp`) gives 700 and 600 or stricter. Windows: `icacls %USERPROFILE%\.ssh\config` lists only the user, SYSTEM and Administrators. Decisive on every OS: `ssh -G wsu-grid` exits 0 without a 'Bad owner' line.
- **Looks like:** `Bad owner or permissions on C:\\Users\\<user>/.ssh/config`; `Bad owner or permissions on /home/<user>/.ssh/config`
- **Repair:** POSIX: `chmod 700 ~/.ssh && chmod 600 ~/.ssh/config`. Windows: `icacls "%USERPROFILE%\.ssh\config" /inheritance:r /grant:r "%USERNAME%:F"`. Both touch only the user's own files.
- **By hand:** Fix the permissions with the commands above, then rerun the doctor.
- **Could break when:** Win32-OpenSSH tightening its ACL checks further.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:303-313; docs/research/deep_research_2026-09-28.json (VS Code topic: Win32-OpenSSH ACL finding)

### `laptop.ssh-key-pair` Grid key pair present, matching, unencrypted, protected

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-keygen`, `laptop.ssh-dir-permissions`, `vscode.settings`

- **Assumes:** ashley.identityFile (default ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu) and its .pub exist and match. The key has no passphrase, because BatchMode must never prompt. The private key is owner-only.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh-keygen -y -P "" -f ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu` exits 0, and the first two fields of its output equal the first two fields of id_ed25519_wsu.pub.
- **Looks like:** `incorrect passphrase supplied to decrypt private key`; `Load key "...id_ed25519_wsu": invalid format`; `WARNING: UNPROTECTED PRIVATE KEY FILE!`; `Permissions for '...id_ed25519_wsu' are too open.`; `no such identity`
- **Repair:** If only the .pub is missing, regenerate it with `ssh-keygen -y -f <key> > <key>.pub`. If only the permissions are wrong, run chmod 600 (POSIX) or the icacls grant (Windows). A mismatch or a passphrase has no automatic fix: a new key needs the password and code once.
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Reset this laptop' > SSH key, then the wizard again. The grid asks for the password and the six-digit code once.
- **Could break when:** Low: only key format changes in OpenSSH.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:274-292; extension/src/extension.ts:470-478; extension/package.json:288-292

### `laptop.ssh-config-block` Ashley's managed block in ~/.ssh/config is present, current and first

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-dir-permissions`, `vscode.settings`, `account.wsu-access-id`, `laptop.openssh`

- **Assumes:** ~/.ssh/config starts with the block between '# >>> ashley (managed; ...' and '# <<< ashley <<<' produced by renderBlock(). 'Host wsu-grid grid' maps to HostName warrior.grid.wayne.edu with User <AccessID>, IdentityFile ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu, IdentitiesOnly yes, PreferredAuthentications publickey,keyboard-interactive,password, GSSAPIAuthentication no, AddressFamily inet, MACs +hmac-sha1, ServerAliveInterval 60, ServerAliveCountMax 5 and ConnectTimeout 20. 'Host *.nas.grid.wayne.edu' adds ProxyJump wsu-grid and StrictHostKeyChecking accept-new. The block is inserted first so it wins over a student's own 'Host *'. Remote-SSH, cli/ashley, cli/ashley.ps1, tools/push_via_grid.sh and testConnection() pass no MAC option and rely on the block. The extension's own calls repeat most options (SSH_BATCH_OPTS).
- **Check:** On the laptop, with no network: first run the wizard's own test, hasBlock(text) && upsertBlock(text, targets(settings)) === text, on ~/.ssh/config. Then `ssh -G wsu-grid` must print 'hostname warrior.grid.wayne.edu', 'user <AccessID>', 'addressfamily inet' and a 'macs' line ending in hmac-sha1, and `ssh -G asx1.nas.grid.wayne.edu` must print 'proxyjump wsu-grid'. Pass = every line matches. Verified on Greg's laptop 2026-09-28.
- **Looks like:** `block missing or outdated`; `ssh: Could not resolve hostname wsu-grid: No such host is known.`; `Unable to negotiate with <ip> port 22: no matching MAC found. Their offer: hmac-sha1`; `every ssh call takes ~13 s (AddressFamily line missing)`; `Bad owner or permissions on .../.ssh/config`; `<config> line N: Bad configuration option: ...`; `Could not resolve hostname grid`
- **Repair:** Run stepSshConfig.run(). It rewrites only the managed block (upsertBlock), keeps ~/.ssh/config.ashley.bak and is idempotent, so it is safe to run unattended.
- **By hand:** Run the wizard's 'SSH configuration' step, or print the block with `node dist/ashley-cli.js ssh-config` and paste it at the TOP of ~/.ssh/config. Do not edit inside the markers. On Windows, if OpenSSH complains about permissions, remove inherited ACL entries on %USERPROFILE%/.ssh/config so that only your own user has access.
- **Could break when:** OpenSSH turning deprecated options into errors; Windows OpenSSH tightening its ACL checks on config files; Wayne State renaming the login host or the node domain. The defaults are hardcoded in store.ts:27-30, cli/main.ts:40-42 and the package.json settings.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:14-60,85-98; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:139-145,294-314; extension/src/core/shell.ts:94-96; cli/ashley:78-88; cli/ashley.ps1:1-12; tools/push_via_grid.sh:13-15

### `laptop.ssh-config-scope` The student's own ssh settings keep their meaning after the block is inserted

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-config-block`

- **Assumes:** upsertBlock puts the block first and appends the student's old file unchanged. ssh_config scopes each line to the most recent Host or Match, so top-level lines in the old file now fall under 'Host *.nas.grid.wayne.edu' and apply only to compute nodes. Examples: 'Include config.d/*', 'AddKeysToAgent yes', and the Include lines that OrbStack or 1Password write. This is safe only when the old file starts with Host or Match. It is traced in code (defect); sshConfig.test.ts has no case for top-level directives.
- **Check:** Laptop: in ~/.ssh/config, the first non-blank, non-comment line after the end marker starts with 'Host ' or 'Match ' (case-insensitive), or the file ends there. Confirm by comparing `ssh -G <one of the student's hosts>` with the output from ~/.ssh/config.ashley.bak (`ssh -F ~/.ssh/config.ashley.bak -G <host>`).
- **Looks like:** `ssh: Could not resolve hostname <alias from an Include file> (after Ashley setup)`; `options that used to apply everywhere (AddKeysToAgent, ServerAliveInterval) now apply only to *.nas.grid.wayne.edu`
- **Repair:** Rewrite the managed block with a final 'Match all' line before the end marker, so the student's later lines are global again. It is a text-only change, config.ashley.bak is kept, and the idempotence test needs updating.
- **By hand:** Move the top-level lines (Include, global options) above the ashley block, or add 'Match all' directly after it.
- **Could break when:** More tools (OrbStack, Colima, 1Password SSH agent) add a top-level Include to ~/.ssh/config.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:85-93; extension/src/core/sshConfig.test.ts:28-35

### `laptop.ssh-known-hosts-login` Login node host key known and verified

Depends on: `laptop.home-directory`, `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** warrior.grid.wayne.edu's host key is in ~/.ssh/known_hosts before the first BatchMode call. The wsu-grid block does not set StrictHostKeyChecking, so the default 'ask' fails under BatchMode. Only the terminal fallback (StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new) records the key. The in-panel ssh2 login has no hostVerifier: it neither records nor checks the host key, so the grid password and code go to whichever host answers. After a successful in-panel install, testConnection can therefore fail on the host key, and the wizard asks for the password and code a second time in a terminal (traced in code, not observed).
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh-keygen -F warrior.grid.wayne.edu` exits 0. The fingerprint from `ssh-keyscan -t ed25519 warrior.grid.wayne.edu | ssh-keygen -lf -` matches a fingerprint pinned in the extension (none is pinned today).
- **Looks like:** `Host key verification failed.`; `WARNING: REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED!`; `A terminal will ask for your grid PASSWORD ... (right after the in-panel login reported 'key installed')`
- **Repair:** With a pinned fingerprint shipped in the extension: append the ssh-keyscan line when it matches the pin, and pass a hostVerifier to ssh2 that checks the same pin. Without a pin there is no safe automatic repair.
- **By hand:** On the campus network, run `ssh -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new wsu-grid true` once, or confirm the fingerprint with hpc@wayne.edu.
- **Could break when:** C&IT reinstalling or replacing the login node (CentOS 7 retirement) changes the host key, and every laptop's BatchMode calls fail at once.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:34-47; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:47-66; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:326-357

### `laptop.ssh-known-hosts-nodes` Compute node host keys accepted and stable

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-config-block`

- **Assumes:** Compute node keys are accepted on first use (StrictHostKeyChecking accept-new in the node block) and stay the same between connections.
- **Check:** Laptop, only to a node where the student has a running job: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu true` shows no 'REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED' on stderr, and `ssh-keygen -F <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu` lists at most one key per type.
- **Looks like:** `WARNING: REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED!`; `Host key for asx1.nas.grid.wayne.edu has changed and you have requested strict checking.`; `Remote-SSH: Could not establish connection ... Host key verification failed`
- **Repair:** No silent repair: removing a changed key is safe only after C&IT confirms a reimage. After the user confirms, run `ssh-keygen -R <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu`.
- **By hand:** Check with hpc@wayne.edu whether the node was reinstalled, then run `ssh-keygen -R <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu`.
- **Could break when:** An OS migration or node reimage rotates every node key at once.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:48-59

### `laptop.default-browser` A browser opens for sign-ins and token pages

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** vscode.env.openExternal opens the default browser, and the CLI uses open, xdg-open or `cmd /c start`. The Microsoft, GitHub and claude.ai sign-ins, the Hugging Face token page and the KB links depend on it.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host, only on an explicit Troubleshoot click: openExternal(Uri.parse('https://ondemand.grid.wayne.edu/')) resolves true. For the CLI: `which xdg-open` (Linux) or `which open` (macOS) exits 0.
- **Looks like:** `openExternal resolved false`; `xdg-open: no method available for opening`; `sign-in waits until the student cancels`
- **By hand:** Set a default browser in the OS settings. Otherwise copy the URL from the Ashley log into a browser.
- **Could break when:** VS Code moving Microsoft sign-in to a native broker (WAM on Windows), which changes where the sign-in appears.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/ui.ts:38-40; extension/src/vscode/panel.ts:77-79; extension/src/cli/terminalUi.ts:59-68

### `laptop.clipboard` The panel can read the clipboard

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** While a token prompt is open, the panel polls vscode.env.clipboard.readText() every second to spot a copied hf_ or sk-ant-oat token.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: save the clipboard, writeText('ashley-probe'), check readText() === 'ashley-probe', then restore it.
- **Looks like:** `'Found a token on your clipboard' never appears`; `readText throws (swallowed at panel.ts:238-240)`
- **By hand:** Paste the token into the field in the panel.
- **Could break when:** Wayland or OS clipboard-access prompts.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/panel.ts:228-247

### `laptop.no-conflicting-env` No conflicting credentials in the laptop's environment

- **Assumes:** The OS or user environment does not already set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN. Either would make the local Claude Code bill or authenticate with that value instead of the subscription. GITHUB_TOKEN, GH_TOKEN and HF_TOKEN are not set there either, so they cannot fight the ones Ashley injects.
- **Check:** Laptop: in the extension host, process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN are undefined. In a new integrated terminal, `echo $env:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (PowerShell) or `printenv ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` prints nothing. Report variable names only, never values.
- **Looks like:** `Claude Code shows API usage billing / apiKeySource ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; `gh or git act as a different GitHub account`
- **By hand:** Remove the variable from the user environment (Windows: System Properties > Environment Variables; macOS/Linux: the shell profile), then restart VS Code.
- **Could break when:** Claude Code changing which credential wins.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:57-86; extension/package.json:345-349; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:117-126

### `laptop.os-keyring` OS keychain backend available for VS Code secrets

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** VS Code SecretStorage has a working OS backend through Electron safeStorage: DPAPI on Windows, Keychain on macOS, and an unlocked libsecret or kwallet on Linux. keytar was removed in VS Code 1.83.
- **Check:** Laptop: vscode.secret-storage round-trips. On Linux, `secret-tool search xdg:schema org.freedesktop.Secret.Generic` exits 0.
- **Looks like:** `An OS keyring couldn't be identified for storing the encryption related data in your current desktop environment.`; `tokens show 'missing' after a Windows password reset (DPAPI master key lost)`; `repeated macOS keychain prompts for 'Code'`
- **By hand:** Linux: install and unlock gnome-keyring, or set "password-store": "gnome-libsecret" in argv.json (Preferences: Configure Runtime Arguments). Windows or macOS after a reset: re-enter the tokens with 'Ashley: Manage stored tokens'.
- **Could break when:** VS Code changing its safeStorage fallback policy (basic_text). Linux desktop keyring changes.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:42-49; docs/INSTALL.md:23; docs/research/deep_research_2026-09-28.json (SecretStorage findings)

### `laptop.node-runtime` Optional system Node >= 20 (MCP runtime preference, CLI requirement)

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** The extension does not need a system Node. A `node` >= 20 on PATH is preferred as the MCP runtime (nodeForMcp), and dist/ashley-cli.js requires it (esbuild target node20, global fetch). Maintainers building need Node 22.
- **Check:** Laptop: `node --version` prints vNN with NN >= 20 for CLI users, NN >= 22 for maintainers. A missing node passes for extension-only users.
- **Looks like:** `'node' is not recognized as an internal or external command`; `ReferenceError: fetch is not defined (Node < 18)`; `MCP registered with 'node' that later resolves to an older nvm version`
- **Repair:** Offer `winget install OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS` or `brew install node` only with consent. Nothing is installed silently.
- **By hand:** Install Node LTS from nodejs.org, or use the extension (it runs the MCP server on VS Code's own runtime).
- **Could break when:** Node 20 reached end of life in April 2026 but nodeForMcp still accepts it. The MCP SDK or esbuild may raise their minimum Node.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/mcpRegister.ts:19-23; extension/esbuild.mjs:10-21; extension/src/cli/main.ts:1-8

### `laptop.cli-local-store` CLI settings and secrets files readable (ashley-cli only)

Depends on: `laptop.node-runtime`, `laptop.home-directory`

- **Assumes:** ~/.ashley-local/settings.json and secrets.json are valid JSON. secrets.json has mode 600, which Windows ignores (the file inherits the profile ACL). Malformed JSON silently falls back to the defaults (the catch in loadJson).
- **Check:** Laptop: `node -e "JSON.parse(require('fs').readFileSync(require('os').homedir()+'/.ashley-local/settings.json','utf8'))"` exits 0 (a missing file is acceptable). On POSIX, secrets.json has mode 600.
- **Looks like:** `the CLI suddenly behaves as if never set up (accessId '')`; `SyntaxError: Unexpected token ... in JSON (hidden by loadJson)`
- **Repair:** On POSIX: chmod 600 ~/.ashley-local/secrets.json.
- **By hand:** Fix or delete the malformed file, then run `node dist/ashley-cli.js setup` again.
- **Could break when:** None from vendors.
- **Code:** extension/src/cli/main.ts:18-32,62-67

### `laptop.cli-terminal` ashley-cli runs in an interactive TTY

Depends on: `laptop.node-runtime`

- **Assumes:** ashley-cli prompts through readline and mutes password echo by overriding stdout.write, and interactive children use stdio 'inherit'. Both need a real TTY.
- **Check:** Laptop, in the shell the student uses: `node -e "process.exit(process.stdin.isTTY&&process.stdout.isTTY?0:1)"` exits 0.
- **Looks like:** `prompts hang when stdin is piped`; `the password is echoed in mintty (Git Bash) without winpty`
- **By hand:** Run the CLI from Windows Terminal, PowerShell or Terminal.app, or use `winpty node ...` in Git Bash.
- **Could break when:** Changes to Node's readline.
- **Code:** extension/src/cli/terminalUi.ts:14-49,69-76

### `laptop.powershell-forwarder` cli/ashley.ps1 may run (execution policy)

Depends on: `laptop.openssh-on-path`, `laptop.ssh-config-block`

- **Assumes:** For Windows users of cli/ashley.ps1: the PowerShell execution policy allows local scripts, and ssh plus the wsu-grid alias work.
- **Check:** Laptop, PowerShell: `Get-ExecutionPolicy` is not Restricted or AllSigned.
- **Looks like:** `ashley.ps1 cannot be loaded because running scripts is disabled on this system.`
- **Repair:** With the user's consent: `Set-ExecutionPolicy -Scope CurrentUser RemoteSigned` (user scope, no admin rights needed).
- **By hand:** Run the command above, or call `ssh wsu-grid ~/.ashley/bin/ashley <verb>` directly.
- **Could break when:** WSU-managed laptops enforcing AllSigned by Group Policy.
- **Code:** cli/ashley.ps1:1-12

### `laptop.ssh-binary-consistency` Every Ashley path uses an ssh that reads the same configuration

Depends on: `laptop.openssh`

- **Assumes:** Up to three ssh executables can be involved. spawn('ssh') resolved through PATH serves the extension, the relay, the MCP server and the CLI. resolveExecutable() uses %SystemRoot%/System32/OpenSSH/ssh.exe for interactive terminals. Remote-SSH uses remote.SSH.path. All of them must read %USERPROFILE%/.ssh/config and known_hosts and support the options in use. On Greg's laptop, PATH resolves only System32/OpenSSH/ssh.exe (9.5p2).
- **Check:** On the laptop: PowerShell `Get-Command ssh -All | % Source` (note the first entry and the count), then `ssh -V` for each entry. The VS Code setting remote.SSH.path must be empty or equal to the first entry. Pass = a single ssh, or all of them at 8.x or newer reading the same home directory. WARN when several different ones are present.
- **Looks like:** `spawn ssh ENOENT (run() returns code 127)`; `the panel works but Remote-SSH does not, or the reverse`; `Git Bash's ssh reads a different HOME and misses the block`
- **By hand:** Put %SystemRoot%/System32/OpenSSH first on PATH, or install Windows' 'OpenSSH Client' optional feature. Set remote.SSH.path to the same ssh.exe.
- **Could break when:** Windows Update silently changing Win32-OpenSSH defaults; Git for Windows putting a newer ssh earlier on PATH.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:30-79,103-113; extension/src/vscode/ui.ts:47-48,84-98; extension/src/core/relay.ts:40; extension/src/mcp/server.ts:18-22

### `laptop.claude-setup-token` claude setup-token mints the token and hands it over

Depends on: `vscode.claude-code-extension`

- **Assumes:** `claude setup-token` needs a TTY (it prints nothing without one; probed), opens claude.ai in the browser for Authorize, and prints a one-year sk-ant-oat... token. The panel runs it in a hidden terminal and captures the first match of /sk-ant-oat[A-Za-z0-9_\-]{20,}/ in the stream after stripping ANSI codes. This has not been run with a real Authorize click. If the CLI's renderer hard-wraps the long token at the terminal width, the match stops at the wrap and a truncated token is stored, which would show up as HTTP 401. Not verified.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `& <claude.exe> setup-token --help` exits 0. After a capture, the token validates (account.claude-oauth-token) and its length equals that of the token printed in the terminal.
- **Looks like:** `If the browser page shows a code, paste it into the terminal that just opened below.`; `'Token captured from the terminal.' followed by 'the service rejected it (HTTP 401)'`; `spawn <path>/claude.exe ENOENT`
- **Repair:** Fall back to clipboard capture (existing). Candidate code fix: remove line breaks inside the match window before applying the pattern.
- **By hand:** Run `claude setup-token` in any terminal, copy the whole token and paste it into the panel.
- **Could break when:** Claude Code renaming setup-token, changing the token prefix or the flow (for example a device code), or moving the native binary.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:412-428; extension/src/vscode/panel.ts:103-176; extension/src/core/services.ts:58-71; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:27-28,42-48,171

### `laptop.ssh-mac-hmac-sha1` ssh client still offers hmac-sha1

Depends on: `laptop.openssh`

- **Assumes:** The laptop's OpenSSH can still negotiate hmac-sha1, the only MAC the grid's OpenSSH 7.4 sshd offers modern clients. 'MACs +hmac-sha1' only appends to the list; it cannot bring back a MAC the build removed.
- **Check:** laptop: `ssh -Q mac` includes a line `hmac-sha1`. Pass = present.
- **Looks like:** `no matching MAC found. Their offer: hmac-sha1`; `Bad SSH2 Mac spec 'hmac-sha1'`
- **By hand:** Install an OpenSSH build that still has hmac-sha1, or ask hpc@wayne.edu to enable hmac-sha2-256/512 on warrior's sshd.
- **Could break when:** An OpenSSH release (Windows OpenSSH follows upstream) that compiles out SHA-1 MACs. Then every grid ssh fails at once until C&IT upgrades sshd.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:43,55; extension/src/core/shell.ts:96

### `laptop.ssh-path-consistency` One ssh binary for Node, terminals and Remote-SSH

Depends on: `laptop.openssh`

- **Assumes:** Several things resolve ssh separately: run('ssh') and run('scp') resolve through the extension host's PATH, while terminals use System32\OpenSSH and Remote-SSH uses remote.SSH.path. All of them must reach the same OpenSSH, reading the same %USERPROFILE%\.ssh\config. A Git-for-Windows/MSYS ssh or a PuTTY plink found first on PATH reads a different config and known_hosts.
- **Check:** laptop: compare `where ssh` (Windows) or `command -v ssh` with resolveExecutable('ssh') and remote.SSH.path. `ssh -G wsu-grid` must print `macs ...hmac-sha1` and `addressfamily inet`. Pass = one binary, and the block is applied.
- **Looks like:** `ssh: Could not resolve hostname wsu-grid`; `no matching MAC found (from a different ssh than the one diagnosed)`
- **Repair:** Set remote.SSH.path to the resolved System32 OpenSSH. Spawn ssh/scp by the absolute path from resolveExecutable.
- **By hand:** Remove the other ssh from PATH, or point remote.SSH.path at C:\Windows\System32\OpenSSH\ssh.exe.
- **Could break when:** Git for Windows or another installer moving its ssh ahead of System32 on PATH.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:30-79,103-113; extension/src/vscode/ui.ts:84-97

### `laptop.scp-sftp-tilde` scp upload to ~/.ashley/bin works

Depends on: `network.grid-login`, `grid.shell-startup-silent`

- **Assumes:** scp from OpenSSH 9+ uses the SFTP protocol by default. Against the grid's OpenSSH 7.4 sftp-server, which has no expand-path extension, the remote path '~/.ashley/bin/' must still resolve, and the sftp subsystem must be enabled.
- **Check:** laptop: scp a 1-byte temp file to wsu-grid:~/.ashley/.scp-probe with BatchMode, then `ssh wsu-grid rm ~/.ashley/.scp-probe`. Pass = exit 0.
- **Looks like:** `scp: realpath ~/.ashley/bin/: No such file`; `subsystem request failed on channel 0`; `Received message too long`; `scp: Connection closed`
- **Repair:** Retry the upload with `scp -O` (legacy protocol), or stream a tar over ssh stdin (`tar -C dist/grid -cf - . | ssh wsu-grid 'tar -C ~/.ashley/bin -xf -'`).
- **By hand:** Run the grid install step again after the fallback. If sftp is disabled on warrior, contact hpc@wayne.edu.
- **Could break when:** OpenSSH removing the legacy scp protocol (-O). C&IT disabling the sftp subsystem.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:111-113; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:534

### `laptop.ssh-key-unencrypted` Grid key loads without a passphrase

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-key`

- **Assumes:** ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu has no passphrase. BatchMode=yes ssh, the relay, and Remote-SSH background connections cannot answer a passphrase prompt. stepSshKey keeps an existing key at that path without inspecting it.
- **Check:** laptop: `ssh-keygen -y -P "" -f ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu` exits 0.
- **Looks like:** `Load key ... incorrect passphrase supplied to decrypt private key`; `Permission denied (publickey,keyboard-interactive) with an agent-less BatchMode`
- **By hand:** Remove the passphrase (`ssh-keygen -p -f ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu -N ""`), or delete the key and rerun the SSH key and login steps.
- **Could break when:** A student reusing a passphrase-protected key, or a security policy requiring passphrases.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:274-292

### `laptop.ssh-key-permissions` Private key file permissions

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-key`

- **Assumes:** The private key is readable only by the student. POSIX mode 600; on Windows, an ACL without inherited Users or Everyone entries. ssh refuses a key that others can read. The wizard only runs chmod on non-Windows systems.
- **Check:** laptop: `ssh-keygen -y -P "" -f key` does not print 'UNPROTECTED PRIVATE KEY FILE'. On Windows, `icacls key` lists only the user, SYSTEM and Administrators.
- **Looks like:** `WARNING: UNPROTECTED PRIVATE KEY FILE!`; `Permissions for '...id_ed25519_wsu' are too open`; `bad permissions: ignore key`
- **Repair:** POSIX: chmod 600 key. Windows: icacls key /inheritance:r /grant:r "%USERNAME%:F".
- **By hand:** Fix the key's permissions as above, or regenerate the key.
- **Could break when:** Profile migration, backup restore or OneDrive/roaming sync resetting ACLs.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:287-289

### `laptop.known-hosts-login` Login node host key known and unchanged

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-config`

- **Assumes:** known_hosts holds warrior's current host key. The login Host block sets no StrictHostKeyChecking, so a BatchMode connection to an unknown host fails. The in-panel ssh2 key install never writes known_hosts, so right after it testConnection can fail and fall back to the terminal.
- **Check:** laptop: `ssh-keygen -F warrior.grid.wayne.edu` finds an entry, and `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` stderr does not contain 'Host key verification failed' or 'REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED'.
- **Looks like:** `Host key verification failed.`; `WARNING: REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED!`; `No ED25519 host key is known for warrior.grid.wayne.edu`
- **Repair:** Missing entry only: one `ssh -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` (trust on first use). Never auto-replace a changed key.
- **By hand:** Changed key: confirm the new fingerprint with hpc@wayne.edu, then `ssh-keygen -R warrior.grid.wayne.edu` and reconnect.
- **Could break when:** C&IT reinstalling or upgrading the login node (el7 to el8/9 regenerates host keys), or adding login nodes behind the same name.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:34-47; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:147-151,326-356; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:47-66

### `laptop.known-hosts-nodes` Compute node host keys

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-config`

- **Assumes:** Node keys are accepted on first use (accept-new) and stay stable. A reimaged node presents a changed key, and Remote-SSH then fails with no prompt.
- **Check:** laptop: after a Remote-SSH failure, `ssh -o BatchMode=yes <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu true` stderr does not contain 'REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED'.
- **Looks like:** `WARNING: REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED!`; `Host key for asx1.nas.grid.wayne.edu has changed`
- **By hand:** Confirm the node was reimaged (hpc@wayne.edu), then `ssh-keygen -R <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu`.
- **Could break when:** Node OS reimages during a Wayne State upgrade.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:48-60

### `laptop.ssh2-js-client` In-panel ssh2 client negotiates with the grid

Depends on: `network.tcp22-banner`

- **Assumes:** The bundled ssh2@1.17.0 (pure JS; cpu-features left external) supports the grid's kex, host key type and hmac-sha1. The password-and-code login in the panel depends on it.
- **Check:** laptop: `node dist/ashley-cli.js ssh2-probe` (probeWithKey) returns 'ssh2 reached'. Pass = exit 0.
- **Looks like:** `Handshake failed: no matching key exchange`; `Handshake failed: no matching host key format`; `Timed out while waiting for handshake`
- **By hand:** The wizard falls back to a terminal login. Rebuild with a newer ssh2.
- **Could break when:** C&IT restricting sshd to post-quantum or other KEX that ssh2 lacks. An ssh2 major version dropping hmac-sha1.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:47-66,109-135; extension/esbuild.mjs:25-29

### `laptop.ssh2-host-key-unverified` In-panel login verifies warrior's host key

Depends on: `laptop.known-hosts-login`

- **Assumes:** The ssh2 connect sets no hostVerifier, so it sends the grid password and the OTP to whatever host answers on port 22. This is safe only on an untampered network path.
- **Check:** code review / laptop: ConnectConfig.hostVerifier compares against the known_hosts entry for warrior. Pass = present.
- **Looks like:** `(silent) credentials sent to an intercepting host`
- **By hand:** Add a hostVerifier that pins the known_hosts key or a fingerprint published by C&IT. Until then, do the first login only from campus or the VPN.
- **Could break when:** Hostile public Wi-Fi. A host key rotation after an upgrade would make a pinned verifier fail, which is correct behaviour.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:47-66

### `laptop.no-anthropic-api-key-env` No ANTHROPIC_API_KEY on the laptop

Depends on: `laptop.os`

- **Assumes:** Nothing in the environment (system variables, the VS Code process, integrated terminals) sets ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN or ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL. If one is set, the local Claude Code silently bills an API key or is redirected. Ashley never injects them, but the student's OS may.
- **Check:** laptop: process.env of the extension host, plus the environmentVariableCollection, contain none of the three.
- **Looks like:** `local Claude shows API usage billing`; `Investigate with Claude charged to an API key`

- **Could break when:** Other tools' installers setting ANTHROPIC_API_KEY globally.

### `laptop.allowlist-source` Pre-approved AccessID list loads

Depends on: `vscode.version`

- **Assumes:** media/allowlist.json in the VSIX parses as {accessIds:[...]}, or ashley.allowlistUrl answers JSON within 10 s. If both fail, allowlist() returns null, which means no restriction; a broken list silently opens the gate.
- **Check:** laptop: read and parse the bundled file. If allowlistUrl is set, fetch it and require HTTP 200 with a JSON accessIds array. Warn when the result would be null.
- **Looks like:** `allowlist null (gate open)`; `<id> is not on the lab's pre-approved list (stale bundled list)`

## VS Code (71)

### `vscode.version` VS Code version supported by Ashley and by the grid's glibc workaround

Depends on: `laptop.vscode-install-kind`

- **Assumes:** VS Code satisfies engines ^1.100.0. The APIs used include shell-integration execution events (1.93+), authentication.getSession, environmentVariableCollection.description and Terminal.exitStatus. Its Remote-SSH server must still honour the custom-glibc escape hatch the CentOS 7 nodes rely on: VS Code >= 1.99 needs glibc 2.28 unless VSCODE_SERVER_CUSTOM_GLIBC_LINKER is set, with patchelf >= 0.18.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: vscode.version satisfies ^1.100.0. Compare it with the last version that connected to a node successfully, which Ashley records in globalState after each successful connect.
- **Looks like:** `Unable to install extension 'openspineconsortium.ashley-wsu' as it is not compatible with VS Code`; `The remote host may not meet VS Code Server's prerequisites for glibc and libstdc++`; `Exit code 207`; `found GLIBC 2.17`
- **Repair:** If a new VS Code breaks the node connection, set remote.SSH.useExecServer to false, the documented first troubleshooting step.
- **By hand:** Update VS Code. If a new release breaks the grid, install the last working version from code.visualstudio.com/updates and turn off updates until the lead confirms.
- **Could break when:** Microsoft removing the custom-glibc path, or requiring a patchelf newer than /wsu/apps/vscode/patchelf/0.19.1. VS Code ships monthly.
- **Code:** extension/package.json:13-15; grid/bashrc.block:1-6; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:255

- **Check:** Maintainer laptop, in extension/: `npx vsce ls --no-dependencies` lists dist/extension.js, dist/mcp-server.js, dist/ashley-cli.js, dist/grid/VERSION, media/allowlist.json and media/manual.html.

Depends on: `vscode.version`

### `vscode.extension-kind-ui` Ashley runs on the laptop even in Remote-SSH windows

Depends on: `vscode.extension-installed`

- **Assumes:** "extensionKind": ["ui"] keeps Ashley in the laptop's extension host. Without it VS Code would deduce ['workspace'] and install Ashley on the node, where SecretStorage, ~/.ssh and the laptop's ssh are missing. The user must not override this in remote.extensionKind.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host in a Remote-SSH window: vscode.extensions.getExtension('openspineconsortium.ashley-wsu').extensionKind === vscode.ExtensionKind.UI, and remote.extensionKind has no entry for it.
- **Looks like:** `ssh: Could not resolve hostname wsu-grid (Ashley running on the node)`; `tokens missing only in the remote window`
- **Repair:** After confirmation, remove the ashley entry from the user setting remote.extensionKind.

- **Could break when:** Changes to VS Code's extension-kind rules.
- **Code:** extension/package.json:16-18; docs/research/deep_research_2026-09-28.json (extensionKind findings)

### `vscode.bundled-grid-scripts` Grid scripts in the VSIX: complete, LF endings, versioned

Depends on: `vscode.bundle-integrity`, `vscode.version`

- **Assumes:** dist/grid holds ashley, ashleyd.js, phone.js, bootstrap.sbatch, worker.sbatch.tpl, bashrc.block and VERSION. esbuild.mjs rewrites CRLF to LF, and .gitattributes sets eol=lf. VERSION equals the grid/VERSION the build came from (2 today). The build copies ../grid only when that folder exists, so a partial checkout silently ships no scripts.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: every file in <extensionPath>/dist/grid is present, none contains '\r' (fs.readFileSync(f,'latin1').includes('\r') is false), and VERSION is non-empty.
- **Looks like:** `no grid scripts found in <ext>/dist/grid`; `/usr/bin/env: 'bash\r': No such file or directory (on the grid)`; `$'\r': command not found`; `the grid has Ashley v1.0.0; this extension brings v2`; `no grid scripts found in <dir>`; `$'\r': command not found (on the grid)`
- **By hand:** Reinstall a correctly built VSIX. The lead rebuilds from a full checkout.
- **Could break when:** A lead editing grid scripts on Windows reintroduces CRLF before a build that bypasses esbuild.mjs.
- **Code:** extension/esbuild.mjs:51-61; .gitattributes:1; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:458-461,492-498; grid/VERSION

### `vscode.bundled-deps` Bundled ssh2, MCP SDK and zod work in VS Code's runtime

Depends on: `vscode.bundle-integrity`, `vscode.version`

- **Assumes:** ssh2 1.17.0, @modelcontextprotocol/sdk 1.30.1 and zod 4.6.5 are pinned and bundled. ssh2's native pieces (sshcrypto.node, cpu-features) are externalised and absent, so ssh2 falls back to pure JS on the extension host's Electron crypto (BoringSSL), whose cipher and KEX set differs from Node's OpenSSL. The ssh2 handshake to warrior was proved with plain Node 22 (ashley-cli ssh2-probe), not inside VS Code's extension host.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host (Troubleshoot): probeWithKey(loginHost, accessId, <private key>) returns ok with the login node's hostname. Log crypto.getCiphers() (expect aes128-ctr) and crypto.getCurves().
- **Looks like:** `Handshake failed: no matching cipher`; `Handshake failed: no matching key exchange algorithm`; `Timed out while waiting for handshake`; `In-panel login did not work (...); falling back to a terminal.`
- **Repair:** The terminal login path is used automatically when the failure is not an authentication failure.
- **By hand:** Use the terminal login the wizard opens. Report the log to the lead.
- **Could break when:** Electron or BoringSSL dropping algorithms. ssh2 has a single maintainer. MCP SDK or zod breaking changes on the next rebuild (pinned until then).
- **Code:** extension/package.json:383-387; extension/esbuild.mjs:23-30; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:47-66,109-135; extension/node_modules/ssh2/lib/protocol/crypto.js:22-33; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:173

### `vscode.settings` ashley.* settings valid and writable

Depends on: `vscode.extension-installed`

- **Assumes:** Settings are valid: accessId matches ^[a-z]{2}[0-9]{4}$, loginHost and nodeDomain resolve, sshAlias is one token, identityFile uses tilde form, and worker.qos is in its enum. Ashley writes them to the Global target, which must not be policy-locked. No workspace-level value overrides sshAlias or loginHost. Settings Sync may carry accessId and twoFactorDone to a new laptop that has no key.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: validate each value, and check that getConfiguration('ashley').inspect(k).workspaceValue is undefined for accessId, sshAlias, loginHost and nodeDomain.
- **Looks like:** `An AccessID looks like ab1234 (two letters, four digits).`; `ssh: Could not resolve hostname <alias>`; `a workspace setting silently overrides ashley.sshAlias`
- **Repair:** Normalise accessId with normaliseAccessId and rewrite it. Remove workspace overrides after confirmation.
- **By hand:** Open Settings, search 'ashley', and correct the value.
- **Could break when:** WSU renaming hosts, in which case the defaults need a new VSIX.
- **Code:** extension/package.json:265-371; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:21-40; extension/src/core/services.ts:88-93

### `vscode.secret-storage` VS Code SecretStorage keeps tokens across restarts

Depends on: `laptop.os-keyring`, `vscode.extension-installed`

- **Assumes:** context.secrets, under the keys ashley.github, ashley.huggingface, ashley.claudeOauth and ashley.anthropicApiKey, persists across restarts through the OS backend: Windows DPAPI via Electron safeStorage, the macOS Keychain, or libsecret or kwallet on Linux. The CLI instead keeps ~/.ashley-local/secrets.json with mode 600, which is not enforced on Windows.
- **Check:** In the extension host: store 'ashley.__diag' with a random value, read it back, confirm it is equal, then delete it. secretSummary() must report the same flags after a window reload.
- **Looks like:** `tokens show 'missing' in the Ashley view after a restart`; `secrets.store rejected`; `An OS keyring couldn't be identified for storing the encryption related data in your current desktop environment.`; `tokens show 'not set' after a restart`; `Error while decrypting the ciphertext provided to safeStorage.decryptString`
- **By hand:** On Linux, install and unlock gnome-keyring, or start VS Code with a supported --password-store. Then run the token steps again; GitHub is one browser click.
- **Could break when:** VS Code changing its secret backends or flags. A new VS Code profile or --user-data-dir starts empty, and Settings Sync does not carry secrets.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:11,42-55; extension/src/extension.ts:424-439

### `vscode.env-collection` Terminal environment injection works (and where it persists)

Depends on: `vscode.secret-storage`, `vscode.settings`

- **Assumes:** The persistent environmentVariableCollection sets WSU_ACCESS_ID, ASHLEY_GRID and ASHLEY_GRID_HOME in every new integrated terminal, local and Remote-SSH. When ashley.injectTokensIntoTerminals is on it also sets GITHUB_TOKEN, GH_TOKEN and HF_TOKEN. It never sets ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN. Unverified side effect: VS Code keeps persistent collections in its storage database (state.vscdb), so these token values may also sit on disk outside the OS keychain.
- **Check:** Laptop, in a new integrated terminal: ASHLEY_GRID equals the alias, and sha256(GITHUB_TOKEN) equals the hash of the SecretStorage value (never print it). Storage question: count rows in %APPDATA%/Code/User/**/state.vscdb whose value contains 'hf_' (names and counts only).
- **Looks like:** `variables missing in terminals opened before setup (warning icon on the terminal tab)`; `gh: HTTP 401: Bad credentials (a stale GITHUB_TOKEN injected)`; `token prefix found in state.vscdb`
- **Repair:** Call store.hydrateEnvironment(), which clears and re-applies the collection, then run workbench.action.terminal.relaunch on open terminals.
- **By hand:** Close and reopen the terminal. If tokens must not persist on disk, turn off ashley.injectTokensIntoTerminals.
- **Could break when:** VS Code changing how collections persist, or the terminal.integrated.environmentChangesRelaunch default.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:57-86; extension/package.json:345-349; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:215-216

### `vscode.log-surface` Failures reach the Ashley log with enough context

Depends on: `vscode.extension-installed`

- **Assumes:** Every failure lands in the Output channel 'Ashley'. reg() catches command errors, logs 'ERROR <command>: <msg>' and shows a notification with a 'Show log' button. The panel keeps a timestamped log and a 'Show the full log' button. Known gaps: some paths swallow errors (tree access lookup, allowlist fetch, clipboard, .mcp.json parse), GridApi logs the command but not ssh's stderr, and run() records exit codes nowhere.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: run ssh against the alias 'ashley-probe-nonexistent' through the same code path, and assert the channel gains a line with the node id and ssh's stderr.
- **Looks like:** `'Cannot reach the grid' with only the first stderr line`; `an empty tree section with no log line`; `a notification without 'Show log'`
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Check everything' and send the full Ashley log to the lead.
- **Could break when:** None from vendors. It degrades whenever a new feature adds a silent catch, which is the pre-integration rule's job to stop.

### `vscode.shell-integration` Shell integration lets Ashley read the setup-token output

Depends on: `vscode.terminal-process`, `vscode.claude-executable`

- **Assumes:** Several things must hold. The default terminal profile activates shell integration within 6 s: PowerShell, pwsh, bash, zsh or fish (not cmd.exe), with terminal.integrated.shellIntegration.enabled on. executeCommand(executable, args) quotes a path with spaces correctly for that shell. The printed token arrives unbroken: if the CLI wraps the long token in the narrow hidden terminal, only the first fragment matches /sk-ant-oat[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}/ and a truncated token is stored. The flow is untested with a real Authorize click.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: in a hidden terminal, onDidChangeTerminalShellIntegration fires within 6 s. Then executeCommand(<claude path>, ['--version']) and read() yields output matching /[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+/.
- **Looks like:** `This terminal has no shell integration; the token will be picked up from your clipboard instead.`; `the service rejected it (HTTP 401) right after 'Token captured from the terminal.'`; `PowerShell echoes the quoted path instead of running it`
- **Repair:** Fall back to the clipboard/paste path (automatic). Code fix: create the hidden terminal with an explicit shellPath for PowerShell or bash so integration does not depend on the user's profile.
- **By hand:** Copy the token the terminal prints; the panel picks it up from the clipboard.
- **Could break when:** Shell-integration scripts change with VS Code releases. Claude Code could change setup-token's output (a new prefix such as sk-ant-oat02, wrapping, Ink UI).
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/panel.ts:108-175; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:412-428; extension/src/core/services.ts:58-71; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:27-28,171

It belongs to VS Code's OAuth app: signing out of GitHub in VS Code can revoke it, and orgs with SAML SSO must authorise the app.

### `vscode.remote-ssh-settings` Remote-SSH settings agree with Ashley's ssh setup

Depends on: `vscode.remote-ssh-extension`

- **Assumes:** Remote-SSH reads the same ~/.ssh/config (remote.SSH.configFile unset or equal) and uses the same ssh (remote.SSH.path unset or valid). remote.SSH.remotePlatform['<node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu'] is 'linux'. remote.SSH.connectTimeout is >= 300: the node's sshd takes about 25 s and the first connection downloads the server. The default exec-server and local-server modes must work with the sysroot.

- **Repair:** Write connectTimeout 300 and the remotePlatform entry (commandConnect already does). If configFile points elsewhere, offer to add the managed block there too. After repeated server-start failures, set remote.SSH.useExecServer to false.
- **By hand:** Settings > Remote.SSH: set Connect Timeout to 300 and clear Config File.
- **Could break when:** Remote-SSH renaming or removing settings and commands (Remote-SSH 0.92 removed opensshremotesexplorer commands). The exec server becoming mandatory.

### `vscode.remote-ssh-connect` Open my grid files: a VS Code window on a compute node

Depends on: `vscode.remote-ssh-settings`, `vscode.version`, `network.proxyjump-compute-node`, `grid-slurm.worker-job`, `grid-login.bashrc-block`

- **Assumes:** vscode.openFolder with vscode-remote://ssh-remote+<node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu<path> opens a window on a node where the student has a RUNNING job, found with `ashley worker node` or squeue. The node name is a single host, not a Slurm range such as voh[3-4]. The VS Code Server installs in the grid home and starts through the C&IT sysroot that the ~/.bashrc block exports.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=60 <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu hostname` prints the node within 120 s. After a connect, the newest '*Remote - SSH.log' under the VS Code logs folder contains 'Extension host agent started'.
- **Looks like:** `Access denied by pam_slurm_adopt: you have no active jobs on this node`; `Connecting with SSH timed out`; `The VS Code Server failed to start`; `Exit code 207`; `remote host voh[3-4].nas.grid.wayne.edu`
- **Repair:** If no job runs, start a worker (commandWorkerStart with the saved spec, after confirmation). For a stale server, ssh to that node and run `pkill -u $USER -f vscode-server` (the student's own processes only), then reconnect.
- **By hand:** Start a worker first. If a second window hangs, run 'Remote-SSH: Kill VS Code Server on Host...' and reconnect.
- **Could break when:** C&IT changing the pam_slurm_adopt policy or the node domain, or removing /wsu/apps/vscode/sysroot after the OS migration. VS Code Server raising its glibc floor again.

### `vscode.claude-executable` A claude executable that spawn() can run

Depends on: `vscode.claude-code-extension`

- **Assumes:** findClaudeExecutable() returns something spawn() can run without a shell: the bundled native binary, or else a real 'claude' executable on PATH. npm installs a claude.cmd shim, and Node will not spawn .cmd without shell:true: ENOENT through the PATH lookup, EINVAL for an explicit .cmd since the 2024 fix.

- **Repair:** Prefer the bundled binary. On Windows, also try %USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe (the native installer's location) when PATH has only a .cmd shim.
- **By hand:** Install or update the Claude Code extension, or install Claude Code with the native installer.
- **Could break when:** Claude Code distribution changes. Node tightening spawn rules further.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:166-173; extension/src/vscode/mcpRegister.ts:34-63; extension/src/extension.ts:467; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:298

### `vscode.mcp-runtime` The MCP server's runtime (node or Electron-as-node) works

Depends on: `laptop.node-runtime`, `laptop.vscode-install-kind`

- **Assumes:** The registered command runs dist/mcp-server.js. It is either a system `node` >= 20 on the PATH Claude Code sees, or VS Code's own binary (process.execPath) with ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1. The second needs Electron's RunAsNode fuse to stay enabled and the binary path to stay stable.
- **Check:** Laptop: spawn the registered command with the registered env and args ['-e', 'process.stdout.write(process.versions.node)']. It prints a Node version >= 20 within 10 s and opens no VS Code window.
- **Looks like:** `MCP server ashley: Connection closed`; `a VS Code window opens instead of the server (RunAsNode fuse disabled)`; `spawn node ENOENT`; `command path under /snap/code/<old revision>/ or .../AppTranslocation/... no longer exists`
- **Repair:** Run registerMcp() again: it recomputes nodeForMcp and the path.
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Connect the Claude Code extension to the grid (MCP)'.
- **Could break when:** VS Code/Electron disabling the RunAsNode fuse for hardening. Changes in the macOS helper-app layout of process.execPath.

### `vscode.mcp-server-path` The registered MCP server path is the current extension's

Depends on: `vscode.bundle-integrity`

- **Assumes:** The registered args[0] equals <current extensionPath>/dist/mcp-server.js. That path contains the extension version (openspineconsortium.ashley-wsu-<version>), so a VSIX with a new version moves it. isMcpRegistered only checks that an 'ashley' entry exists.
- **Check:** Laptop: read mcpServers.ashley.args[0] from ~/.claude.json (or the output of `claude mcp get ashley`). It equals path.join(context.extensionPath,'dist','mcp-server.js') and the file exists.
- **Looks like:** `Error: Cannot find module 'C:\\Users\\<user>\\.vscode\\extensions\\openspineconsortium.ashley-wsu-1.0.0\\dist\\mcp-server.js'`; `Claude Code lists ashley as failed`
- **Repair:** On activation, if the paths differ: `claude mcp remove --scope user ashley`, then registerMcp().
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Connect the Claude Code extension to the grid (MCP)'.
- **Could break when:** Every Ashley version bump. package.json is still 1.0.0 while grid VERSION is 2, so the first bump will trigger this.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/mcpRegister.ts:25-32,59-63; extension/src/extension.ts:157; extension/package.json:5

### `vscode.mcp-registration` Claude Code has the ashley MCP server (user scope)

Depends on: `vscode.claude-executable`, `vscode.mcp-runtime`, `vscode.mcp-server-path`, `vscode.claude-code-extension`, `vscode.mcp-node-runtime`

- **Assumes:** `claude mcp add-json --scope user ashley '<json>'` stores {type stdio, command, args [<extensionPath>/dist/mcp-server.js], env {ASHLEY_GRID, ASHLEY_WORKER, ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE when needed}}. The fallback writes <workspace>/.mcp.json, which Claude Code asks once to approve. isMcpRegistered() checks only that `claude mcp get ashley` exits 0 and mentions ashley. It does not check that the path exists or that the server connects.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `& <claude.exe> mcp get ashley` exits 0 and reports the server as connected. The args path it prints exists (Test-Path) and lies under the CURRENTLY installed Ashley extension folder, whose version matches package.json.
- **Looks like:** `TODO MCP bridge registered with Claude Code`; `Failed to connect`; `Could not register the MCP bridge: ...`; `the ashley_* tools are missing in a new Claude Code conversation`; `add-json refuses on a second registration (a duplicate name; likely), after which the code writes a project .mcp.json instead of replacing the user entry`; `No MCP server found with name: ashley`; `Failed to connect, after an extension update deleted the old extension folder: isMcpRegistered() still returns true, so setup never registers again`; `registration saved with its backslashes mangled (C:UsersgregoAppData...)`; `a project .mcp.json waiting for approval`
- **Repair:** Run `claude mcp remove --scope user ashley`, then add-json with the current extension path and runtime, spawned with an argv array (no shell). Safe and idempotent.
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Connect the Claude Code extension to the grid (MCP)'. Do not type the JSON by hand in PowerShell: it mangles quotes.
- **Could break when:** Claude Code renaming the mcp subcommands or the --scope values, moving its configuration file, or changing MCP transports.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/mcpRegister.ts:25-63; extension/src/extension.ts:157,207,414-422,467; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:146-149; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:226,296

An existing .mcp.json that fails to parse is silently replaced by {} plus ashley, so its other servers are lost.

- **Could break when:** Changes to Claude Code's project-MCP approval model.

### `vscode.mcp-server-process` The MCP server starts and reaches the grid

Depends on: `vscode.mcp-registration`, `vscode.relay-session`, `laptop.openssh-on-path`, `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** dist/mcp-server.js (bundling @modelcontextprotocol/sdk 1.30.1 and zod 4.6.5) speaks MCP over stdio in a protocol version that the installed Claude Code accepts. It inherits a PATH on which `ssh` resolves, ASHLEY_GRID names a working alias, and it keeps one GridRelay open for its lifetime.
- **Check:** On the laptop, from Node or Git Bash (LF only): pipe one initialize request into the registered command: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"initialize","params":{"protocolVersion":"2025-06-18","capabilities":{},"clientInfo":{"name":"diag","version":"0"}}}. Pass = a response line whose serverInfo.name is 'ashley'. End to end: Claude Code's ashley_status tool returns JSON containing 'ready'.

- **Repair:** Re-register (vscode.mcp-registration); the relay reconnects by itself.

### `vscode.relay-session` The persistent relay to the login node

Depends on: `network.grid-login`, `network.ssh-connection-budget`, `account.grid-key-authorized`, `grid-login.ashley-cli`

- **Assumes:** Each process (extension, MCP server, CLI) keeps one `ssh <alias> -- ~/.ashley/bin/ashley relay`. It answers 'READY <ms> <version>' and speaks SEND/QUEUED, RESULT, PING/PONG and EVENT lines. A dropped session reconnects on the next call, and every call falls back to a fresh ssh. The relay is a long-lived loop on the login node.
- **Check:** Laptop: relay.ping() returns rttMs < 2000 and the READY version equals dist/grid/VERSION. CLI: `node dist/ashley-cli.js latency 1`.
- **Looks like:** `relay closed (ssh exit 255)`; `no QUEUED ack from the relay within 30 s`; `relay is not connected`; `relay send failed (...); using a fresh ssh`
- **Repair:** relay.close() then ensure(). Safe: the file on the grid is the message, so nothing already queued is lost.
- **By hand:** None needed; slow answers mean the fallback ssh path is in use. Send the log if it persists.
- **Could break when:** C&IT killing long-lived login-node processes (a process reaper or cgroup limits), idle-session timeouts (ClientAliveInterval, TMOUT), or sshd MaxSessions.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/relay.ts:35-83,166-205; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:110-199; extension/src/extension.ts:26-32; grid/ashley:389-401; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:50-80

### `vscode.core-remote-quoting` Remote command quoting in ssh() (tilde and $USER defects)

Depends on: `laptop.openssh-on-path`, `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** ssh() renders the argv as one remote command with POSIX single quotes, leaving only a leading '~/' of the FIRST argument unquoted, and the login shell is bash. Three consequences are traced in code and reproduced by emulating the renderer. (1) stepGridInstall.run sends `'mkdir' '-p' '~/.ashley/bin' ...`, which creates a directory literally named '~' in the grid home, so on a fresh account the following scp has no ~/.ashley/bin to copy into. (2) GridApi.squeue sends `'squeue' '-u' '$USER'`, so squeue gets the literal string and squeue() returns []. Connect's fallback, 'Grid status' and ashley_squeue use it. (3) readFile and listDir do not expand '~/...' paths. Strings passed through `bash -c` (pushTokens, sbatch) are expanded, but sbatch splices cwd into that string unquoted. None of this was observed on a fresh account, because Greg's account already had ~/.ashley/bin.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'test -d "$HOME/~" && echo LITERAL_TILDE_DIR'` prints nothing. Unit test: ssh() renders ['mkdir','-p','~/x'] with '~/' unquoted and ['squeue','-u','$USER'] with $USER expanded.
- **Looks like:** `upload failed: scp: ...: No such file or directory (first install on a fresh account)`; `squeue: error: Invalid user: $USER`; `head: cannot open '~/...' for reading: No such file or directory`; `a directory named '~' in the grid home`
- **Repair:** Runtime: before scp, run `ssh wsu-grid 'mkdir -p ~/.ashley/bin ~/.ashley/log ~/.ashley/accounts/default ~/.ashley/workers ~/.ashley/sessions'`. Show the student the contents of "$HOME/~" and remove it only with `rmdir` or `rm -r -- "$HOME/~"`, never `rm -rf ~`. Code fix: render '~/rest' as "$HOME"/'rest' for every argument, and send $USER as "$USER" (or use `id -un`) through bash -c.
- **By hand:** Tell the lead. Never type `rm -rf ~` to clean up the '~' directory: that deletes the whole home.
- **Could break when:** WSU changing the default login shell (tcsh, zsh) would change quoting and expansion again.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:85-108; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:532-535; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:55-58,218-232; extension/src/mcp/server.ts:137-171

- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid '~/.ashley/bin/ashley version'` equals <extensionPath>/dist/grid/VERSION.

Depends on: `vscode.fetch-network`, `external.github-api`, `external.huggingface-api`, `external.anthropic-oauth-probe`

The daily check (20 s after start, at most once per 24 h) only warns on rejections. This works only while every vendor endpoint keeps those status meanings.

- **Repair:** Offer Renew (commandTokenStep), which already exists.
- **By hand:** Press Renew next to the token in the panel.
- **Could break when:** A vendor answering 403 to a valid token on these endpoints would make Ashley nag every student every day.

### `vscode.ssh2-inpanel-login` In-panel password + code login installs the key

Depends on: `vscode.bundled-deps`, `network.grid-login`, `network.sshd-algorithms`, `account.grid-password`, `account.grid-2fa-authenticator`, `laptop.ssh-key-pair`

- **Assumes:** installPublicKey connects to loginHost:22 with ssh2 using keyboard-interactive. Prompts are routed by /pass/i (the password) and /verification|code|token/i (the Authenticator code). HMACs are sha2-256, sha2-512 and sha1, with a 120 s timeout and three attempts. It appends the key to ~/.ssh/authorized_keys under umask 077. Nothing is stored, and the host key is not verified (see laptop.ssh-known-hosts-login).
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host, once the key is installed: probeWithKey(loginHost, accessId, key) returns ok. The password path cannot be checked without the student.
- **Looks like:** `the grid rejected the password or the code`; `three rejected logins: check the account exists and redo the Authenticator setup`; `timed out talking to the grid`; `unexpected reply: ...`; `In-panel login did not work (...); falling back to a terminal.`
- **Repair:** The terminal login is used automatically for failures that are not authentication failures.
- **By hand:** Retype the password and a fresh six-digit code. After three rejections, redo the Authenticator setup on OnDemand.
- **Could break when:** WSU replacing Google Authenticator with Duo would change the prompt text (e.g. 'Passcode or option (1-3):'). Password-expiry prompts. Adding host-key pinning.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:27-106; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:326-346; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:152-160

### `vscode.phone-flow` Phone access: Remote Control hosted by a worker

Depends on: `grid-slurm.worker-job`, `grid-login.ashley-cli`, `laptop.default-browser`, `laptop.clipboard`, `vscode.webview-panel`

- **Assumes:** commandPhone runs `ashley phone on --worker <w>` and polls `ashley phone status --json` every 2.5 s for phase need_code, running or error. It opens the claude.ai URL the worker reports, captures the code the student copies (/^[A-Za-z0-9_\-#.~]{20,}$/), and sends it with `ashley phone code` over stdin. The worker must already be RUNNING.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid '~/.ashley/bin/ashley phone status --worker default --json'` exits 0 with JSON containing a phase.
- **Looks like:** `Phone access runs on a Claude worker, and none is running.`; `Phone access did not start: <message>`; `Phone access is waiting for the sign-in`
- **By hand:** Start a worker, then run 'Ashley: Phone access' again and complete the claude.ai sign-in.
- **Could break when:** Claude Code Remote Control is a vendor feature whose sign-in and code flow can change. The sign-in lasts about 30 days.
- **Code:** extension/src/extension.ts:531-577; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:88-102

- **Check:** In the extension host: `vscode.authentication.getSession('microsoft', ['VSCODE_TENANT:organizations','User.Read'], { silent: true })` resolves without throwing. An undefined result only means the student is not signed in yet and is not a failure. `vscode.extensions.getExtension('vscode.microsoft-authentication')` is defined. Log the value of the setting microsoft-authentication.implementation.

- **By hand:** Use Microsoft's build of VS Code, and sign in with AccessID@wayne.edu rather than a personal account.
- **Could break when:** VS Code changing its Microsoft sign-in implementation (MSAL, the Windows broker), the VSCODE_TENANT scope convention, or the format of the account label.

ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN are never injected, because the local Claude Code would then bill the key. VS Code caches a persistent collection in its own storage. Whether the token values sit there in clear text was not checked, so the statement 'tokens live only in the keychain' may not be exact.

Confirmed: Code.exe works as a Node 24 runtime. The absolute path of Code.exe is written into the registration.
