## Network (27)

### `network.grid-login` SSH to the login node with no prompt (BatchMode)

Depends on: `laptop.openssh-on-path`, `laptop.ssh-binary-consistency`, `account.grid-authorized-key`, `account.wsu-password-current`

- **Assumes:** `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid <cmd>` authenticates with the key alone and runs the command within ConnectTimeout 20 s (2.0 to 2.7 s measured from Windows). Every laptop-to-grid path rides on it: GridApi.ashley(), testConnection(), gridInstalled(), pushTokens(), the scp upload, the relay, the MCP server, cli/ashley, cli/ashley.ps1, tools/push_via_grid.sh and Remote-SSH's ProxyJump.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o PubkeyAuthentication=no -o ConnectTimeout=20 wsu-grid true` ends in 'Permission denied (...)' within 25 s. That proves DNS, TCP, banner and key exchange without using credentials. Windows alternative: `Test-NetConnection warrior.grid.wayne.edu -Port 22` gives TcpTestSucceeded True.
- **Looks like:** `ssh: Could not resolve hostname warrior.grid.wayne.edu: No such host is known.`; `ssh: connect to host warrior.grid.wayne.edu port 22: Connection timed out`; `Connection refused`; `kex_exchange_identification: read: Connection reset`; `Permission denied (publickey,keyboard-interactive).`; `Host key verification failed.`; `no matching MAC found. Their offer: hmac-sha1`; `Received disconnect from <ip> port 22:2: Too many authentication failures`; `This account is currently not available.`; `Password change required but no TTY available.`
- **Repair:** Check the children first and repair the first one that fails (block rewrite, first-use host key). Never retry authentication more than twice a minute: the grid counts failed attempts.
- **By hand:** Run Ashley: Check everything and fix the root cause it names. If every local check passes and ssh still fails, check external.grid-maintenance and write to hpc@wayne.edu.
- **Could break when:** A login-node OS migration (CentOS 7 to RHEL 8 or 9) changes host keys, crypto policy and possibly the authentication methods all at once. The login host may also be renamed.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:94-108; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:147-151,464-469; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:37-45; extension/src/vscode/tree.ts:64; cli/ashley:78-88; cli/ashley.ps1:1-12; tools/push_via_grid.sh:13-15

### `network.ipv4-path` IPv4 path used (IPv6 attempt avoided)

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** The IPv4 route to the login node works. From Windows the IPv6 attempt times out first: 13.1 s per connection versus 2.0 s with IPv4, measured over nine runs each. Ashley therefore passes AddressFamily inet in the block, in SSH_BATCH_OPTS and to terminals.
- **Check:** Laptop: time three `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` runs; the median is < 5 s.
- **Looks like:** `every ssh call takes about 13 s`; `the tree view loads slowly`
- **Repair:** Rewrite the managed block (AddressFamily inet).
- **By hand:** Run the wizard step 'SSH configuration' again.
- **Could break when:** An IPv6-only network without CLAT (some hotspots) would fail with AddressFamily inet. WSU fixing IPv6 on the login node would make the option unnecessary.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:27-28,42; extension/src/core/shell.ts:96; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:206-209; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:241

### `network.sshd-algorithms` Laptop ssh/ssh2 and the grid's sshd agree on algorithms

Depends on: `network.grid-login`, `laptop.openssh`

- **Assumes:** The laptop's ssh and ssh2 share KEX, cipher, MAC and host-key algorithms with the grid's sshd (OpenSSH 7.4). From Windows OpenSSH 9.5 the grid negotiated only once 'MACs +hmac-sha1' was added ('no matching MAC found' observed). The research found that a stock 7.4 offers hmac-sha2, so the grid's list is probably restricted by C&IT. Which MACs it offers today is not recorded.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -vv -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true 2>&1` exits 0. Record the 'kex: algorithm:' line and the 'kex: server->client cipher: ... MAC: ...' line in the report.
- **Looks like:** `Unable to negotiate with <ip> port 22: no matching MAC found. Their offer: hmac-sha1`; `no matching key exchange method found`; `no matching host key type found. Their offer: ssh-rsa`
- **Repair:** Add the offered algorithm with '+' in the managed block, but only if it is on an allowlist (hmac-sha1, diffie-hellman-group14-sha1). Never add ssh-dss.
- **By hand:** Send the -vv output to the lead.
- **Could break when:** C&IT upgrading sshd or tightening crypto policy. Windows OpenSSH dropping hmac-sha1. Electron BoringSSL (ssh2) and the grid drifting apart.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:19-20,43; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:53-54; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:241,291; docs/research/deep_research_2026-09-28.json (MACs finding)

### `network.windows-socket-stability` Windows TCP connections to the grid are reliable

Depends on: `laptop.os-platform`

- **Assumes:** Connections from a Windows laptop to the grid succeed consistently. On this project's own Windows machine, sockets have been seen to fail intermittently for git push and ssh, and the standing practice there is to route network work through WSL. The cause is unknown. Ashley deliberately does not use WSL.
- **Check:** Laptop (Windows): run `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` three times; pass if all three exit 0, and record stderr for each run.
- **Looks like:** `kex_exchange_identification: read: Connection reset`; `Connection timed out during banner exchange`; `client_loop: send disconnect: Connection reset`
- **Repair:** Retry with backoff. ssh calls are idempotent, and send is idempotent by request id.
- **By hand:** Record the times of failure and compare with `wsl -e ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` to separate the Windows stack from the network.
- **Could break when:** Updates to the Windows network stack, the VPN client or antivirus.
- **Code:** docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:207-208; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:6-12

### `network.ssh-connection-budget` The login node tolerates Ashley's connection pattern

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** sshd accepts Ashley's pattern without hitting MaxStartups or MaxSessions, a per-source rate limit or fail2ban-style blocking. The pattern is a fresh ssh per call (3–5 per tree refresh, plus the daily token check and the doctor), one long-lived relay per process, and Remote-SSH's own sessions. Failed authentications count against the student (hence IdentitiesOnly).
- **Check:** Laptop: five sequential `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` within 30 s all exit 0.
- **Looks like:** `kex_exchange_identification: Connection closed by remote host`; `Connection timed out (after a burst)`; `Received disconnect from <ip> port 22:2: Too many authentication failures`
- **Repair:** Back off. Code fix: route status calls over the relay.
- **By hand:** Wait a few minutes. If it persists, send the log to the lead.
- **Could break when:** C&IT adding connection throttling, or an OpenSSH >= 9.8 sshd with PerSourcePenalties.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/tree.ts:55-131; extension/src/extension.ts:178-192; extension/src/core/relay.ts:35-71; extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:22-23

### `network.proxyjump-compute-node` ProxyJump through the login node reaches a compute node

Depends on: `network.grid-login`, `account.grid-key-authorized`, `laptop.ssh-config-block`, `laptop.ssh-known-hosts-nodes`, `grid-slurm.worker-job`

- **Assumes:** The login node allows stdio/TCP forwarding (direct-tcpip) and resolves <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu. The node's sshd admits the student only while a job of theirs runs there (pam_slurm_adopt). It takes about 23–25 s before running anything.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=60 <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu hostname`, with the node from `ssh wsu-grid '~/.ashley/bin/ashley worker node --name default'`, prints the node within 120 s.
- **Looks like:** `channel 0: open failed: administratively prohibited: open failed`; `stdio forwarding failed`; `Access denied by pam_slurm_adopt: you have no active jobs on this node`; `ssh: Could not resolve hostname voh3.nas.grid.wayne.edu (resolved on the login node)`
- **Repair:** If no job runs, start a worker. Raise remote.SSH.connectTimeout to 300 (already done).
- **By hand:** Start a worker, wait until it is RUNNING, then connect.
- **Could break when:** C&IT disabling AllowTcpForwarding on login nodes, changing the node domain, or changing the pam_slurm_adopt policy.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:22-30,48-59; extension/src/extension.ts:269-271; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:246,253; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:29-32

### `network.wsu-campus-or-vpn` On the campus network or the WSU VPN (for OnDemand)

- **Assumes:** ondemand.grid.wayne.edu answers only from the campus network or the WSU VPN (GlobalProtect), so any HTTP answer means the laptop is on campus or the VPN. It is needed once, for the Authenticator enrolment (the 2FA Setup app).
- **Check:** Laptop: onDemandReachable() fetches https://ondemand.grid.wayne.edu/ with redirect 'manual' and an 8 s timeout; any HTTP status counts as reachable.
- **Looks like:** `Check again (OnDemand not reachable: join the WSU Wi-Fi on campus, or connect the WSU VPN)`; `fetch failed`; `This operation was aborted`
- **By hand:** Join the campus Wi-Fi, or connect GlobalProtect, then choose 'Check again'.
- **Could break when:** WSU exposing OnDemand publicly (the check becomes meaningless but harmless), renaming the host, or replacing GlobalProtect.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:88,94-105,237-253

### `network.https-egress` Laptop HTTPS egress to the vendor endpoints

- **Assumes:** HTTPS reaches api.github.com, huggingface.co, api.anthropic.com, claude.ai, login.microsoftonline.com, github.com and marketplace.visualstudio.com, possibly through a proxy. Maintainers also need registry.npmjs.org and update.code.visualstudio.com. The system clock must be correct for TLS.
- **Check:** Laptop, extension host: GET each host with a 10 s timeout. Any HTTP status means reachable; TLS errors are reported separately.
- **Looks like:** `fetch failed`; `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`; `certificate has expired`; `certificate is not yet valid (laptop clock wrong)`; `self signed certificate in certificate chain (TLS inspection)`
- **Repair:** Keep tokens and verify them on the grid (existing).
- **By hand:** Fix the network, proxy or clock, then rerun the doctor.
- **Could break when:** Vendor domain changes. Captive portals.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:38,52,66,80; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:361-382; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:92-126

### `network.grid-login-dns` Login host name resolves (IPv4)

- **Assumes:** warrior.grid.wayne.edu (setting ashley.loginHost) resolves to at least one IPv4 address from the student's network. The laptop never resolves compute-node names: with ProxyJump, warrior resolves them.
- **Check:** On the laptop: PowerShell `Resolve-DnsName warrior.grid.wayne.edu -Type A` (POSIX: `getent hosts warrior.grid.wayne.edu`). Pass = one or more A records. Log the addresses, and any AAAA record for network.ssh-ipv4-only.
- **Looks like:** `ssh: Could not resolve hostname warrior.grid.wayne.edu: No such host is known.`; `ssh: Could not resolve hostname warrior.grid.wayne.edu: Name or service not known`; `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND warrior.grid.wayne.edu (in-panel ssh2 login)`
- **Repair:** Flush the resolver cache once and check again: `ipconfig /flushdns` on Windows, `resolvectl flush-caches` on Linux, `dscacheutil -flushcache` on macOS. Nothing else is safe to automate.
- **By hand:** Check the network (captive portal, hotel Wi-Fi). If Wayne State renamed the login host, set ashley.loginHost to the new name and rerun the 'SSH configuration' step.
- **Could break when:** C&IT retiring 'warrior' for a new cluster name, or putting several login nodes behind one name. The default name is hardcoded in store.ts:27, cli/main.ts:40 and package.json (ashley.loginHost).
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:27; extension/src/cli/main.ts:40; extension/package.json:273-277; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:46-50

### `network.grid-login-tcp22` TCP 22 to the login node is reachable from this network

Depends on: `network.grid-login-dns`

- **Assumes:** Port 22 on warrior is reachable from wherever the student is: home, campus Wi-Fi or the WSU VPN. The design assumes SSH faces the internet and that only OnDemand needs the campus network or the VPN. The measurements in CHECKPOINT come from one Windows laptop. Networks that block outbound 22, such as hospital or guest Wi-Fi, were not tested.
- **Check:** On the laptop: PowerShell `Test-NetConnection warrior.grid.wayne.edu -Port 22` must show TcpTestSucceeded True (POSIX: `nc -vz -w 5 warrior.grid.wayne.edu 22`). Also read the server banner, e.g. `ssh -v -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` and look for 'remote software version'. Today it is OpenSSH_7.4. Log it: a change means the login node was rebuilt.
- **Looks like:** `ssh: connect to host warrior.grid.wayne.edu port 22: Connection timed out`; `ssh: connect to host warrior.grid.wayne.edu port 22: Connection refused`; `kex_exchange_identification: read: Connection reset`; `kex_exchange_identification: Connection closed by remote host`; `timed out talking to the grid (in-panel login)`
- **By hand:** Try the WSU VPN (GlobalProtect, portal protect.wayne.edu) or another network. If it fails from every network including the VPN, the grid is probably down for maintenance (see external.grid-maintenance); write to hpc@wayne.edu. After many failed logins the address may be blocked for a while, so wait before retrying.
- **Could break when:** WSU restricting SSH to campus and VPN, a common HPC hardening step. Every off-campus call, the relay and Remote-SSH would then time out, and the wizard's statement that only OnDemand needs the VPN would be wrong. sshd could also move to another port or behind a bastion host.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:94-105,247-256; extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:26; docs/manual/00_getting_started.md; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:241

### `network.ssh-ipv4-only` SSH forced to IPv4 (AddressFamily inet)

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-config-block`

- **Assumes:** On Windows, the IPv6 attempt to warrior times out before IPv4 is tried. Measured 2026-09-28 over nine runs each: 13.1 s per connection with IPv6 allowed, 2.0 s with AddressFamily inet. The block sets it for both Host entries. SSH_BATCH_OPTS, testConnection() and the terminal fallback pass -o AddressFamily=inet. cli/ashley, cli/ashley.ps1 and push_via_grid.sh rely on the block.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `ssh -G wsu-grid` prints 'addressfamily inet', and so does `ssh -G <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu`. Time `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true`: pass = under 5 s (baseline 2.0 to 2.7 s). Optionally, `Resolve-DnsName warrior.grid.wayne.edu -Type AAAA` shows whether an IPv6 address is published.
- **Looks like:** `every grid action takes about 13 s`; `ssh -v shows 'connect to address <IPv6> port 22: Connection timed out' before the IPv4 attempt`
- **Repair:** Rewrite the managed block with stepSshConfig.run(). Safe and idempotent.
- **By hand:** Rerun the wizard's SSH configuration step. Do not put a 'Host *' block that sets AddressFamily above the Ashley block.
- **Could break when:** If WSU fixes IPv6 routing the setting becomes harmless. An IPv6-only login address (unlikely) would break every call. A Remote-SSH update could also pass its own AddressFamily option.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:27-28,42,53; extension/src/core/shell.ts:96; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:148; extension/src/vscode/ui.ts:48; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:241,291

### `network.ssh-mac-hmac-sha1` SHA-1 MAC re-enabled for the grid's sshd

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-config-block`

- **Assumes:** The login node's sshd (OpenSSH 7.4 on CentOS 7) offers only hmac-sha1 to Windows OpenSSH 9.5, whose default MAC list leaves it out. Verified locally: `ssh -G wsu-grid` shows the default list with hmac-sha1 appended by the block. The client must still implement hmac-sha1; OpenSSH_for_Windows_9.5p2 does (`ssh -Q mac`). The in-panel ssh2 client (npm ssh2 1.17.0) offers hmac-sha2-256, hmac-sha2-512 and hmac-sha1. testConnection(), cli/ashley, cli/ashley.ps1 and push_via_grid.sh do not pass the MAC option and depend on the block.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `ssh -Q mac` lists hmac-sha1, and the 'macs' line of `ssh -G wsu-grid` ends in hmac-sha1. After a successful connection, `ssh -vv -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` shows the negotiated MAC in its 'kex: server->client cipher: ... MAC: ...' line. Log it: a switch to hmac-sha2-* means WSU upgraded sshd and the option can go.
- **Looks like:** `Unable to negotiate with <ip> port 22: no matching MAC found. Their offer: hmac-sha1`; `no matching MAC found. Their offer: hmac-sha1`; `Bad SSH2 MAC spec '+hmac-sha1'`; `Handshake failed: no matching mac (ssh2, in-panel login)`
- **Repair:** Rewrite the managed block with stepSshConfig.run(). If the client no longer implements hmac-sha1, there is no safe automatic repair.
- **By hand:** Rerun the SSH configuration step. If Windows OpenSSH has dropped SHA-1 MACs, install another OpenSSH (Git for Windows or a newer Win32-OpenSSH), put it first on PATH and set remote.SSH.path to it. Tell hpc@wayne.edu that the login node needs to offer hmac-sha2.
- **Could break when:** Microsoft removing SHA-1 MACs from Win32-OpenSSH altogether; npm ssh2 dropping hmac-sha1. A login node rebuilt on RHEL 8 or 9 would offer sha2 under its crypto policy, which makes this line harmless.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:19-20,43,55; extension/src/core/shell.ts:96; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:54,133; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:148,352; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:241,291

### `network.grid-login-hostkey` Login node host key known before any BatchMode call

Depends on: `network.grid-login-tcp22`

- **Assumes:** warrior's host key is already in ~/.ssh/known_hosts. The login Host entry sets no StrictHostKeyChecking, so it resolves to 'ask' (confirmed with `ssh -G wsu-grid`), and BatchMode turns 'ask' into a refusal. The in-panel ssh2 login sets no hostVerifier: it neither checks nor records the host key. On a fresh laptop this has a consequence. After a successful in-panel key install, testConnection() fails with 'Host key verification failed.', and stepInstallKey falls through to the terminal login (StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new), which asks for the password and the code a second time. Greg's laptop already had an ssh-rsa entry for warrior (known_hosts line 6), which is why this never showed. Because nothing checks the host key, the ssh2 path also sends the password and one-time code to whichever host answers.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `ssh-keygen -F warrior.grid.wayne.edu` exits 0 and prints an entry. Log its key type (today ssh-rsa). Also `ssh -G wsu-grid` and read its stricthostkeychecking line.
- **Looks like:** `Host key verification failed.`; `WARNING: REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED!`; `Host key for warrior.grid.wayne.edu has changed and you have requested strict checking.`; `still prompted: Host key verification failed. (wizard, right after the in-panel login succeeded)`
- **Repair:** Only when the key is missing, never when it has changed: record it on first use with `ssh -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` (the key is stored before authentication). Better, compare it with a fingerprint published by C&IT. Code changes to consider: add 'StrictHostKeyChecking accept-new' to the login Host entry, and give ssh2 a hostVerifier that checks and writes known_hosts.
- **By hand:** If the key CHANGED, confirm with hpc@wayne.edu that the login node was rebuilt. Then run `ssh-keygen -R warrior.grid.wayne.edu` and connect once in a terminal.
- **Could break when:** An OS migration regenerates host keys. Every laptop then fails at once with REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED, and no automatic repair is safe. Several login nodes behind one name with different keys would cause the same symptom intermittently.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:33-46,58; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:46-66; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:147-151,316-358; extension/src/core/shell.ts:96

### `network.campus-vpn-ondemand` Campus network or WSU VPN (for the OnDemand 2FA setup)

- **Assumes:** ondemand.grid.wayne.edu answers only from the campus network (WSU Wi-Fi) or through the WSU GlobalProtect VPN (portal protect.wayne.edu, with MFA, for current students and employees). onDemandReachable() treats any HTTP answer within 8 s as 'on campus or on the VPN'; it does not follow redirects. This is needed only for the one-time Authenticator setup.
- **Check:** On the laptop. Skip when ashley.twoFactorDone is true or the key already works. Otherwise run `curl.exe -s -o NUL -m 8 -w "%{http_code}" https://ondemand.grid.wayne.edu/`: any code other than 000 (typically 302) means reachable. VPN client state: `Get-NetAdapter | ? InterfaceDescription -match 'PANGP'` shows the adapter Up.
- **Looks like:** `Check again (OnDemand not reachable: join the WSU Wi-Fi on campus, or connect the WSU VPN)`; `fetch failed`; `This operation was aborted (the 8 s timeout)`; `a captive portal answers 200 for everything (false pass)`
- **By hand:** Connect GlobalProtect to protect.wayne.edu, or join the campus Wi-Fi, then choose 'Check again'.
- **Could break when:** If OnDemand opens to the internet behind SSO, the check no longer detects the VPN (harmless for the setup). The OnDemand URL or the VPN portal could be renamed, or the VPN limited to employees.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:88,94-105,236-266; docs/INSTALL.md; docs/RESEARCH.md:367

### `network.ssh2-inpanel-login` The in-panel SSH client (npm ssh2) can talk to the grid

Depends on: `network.grid-login-tcp22`, `account.wsu-access-id`

- **Assumes:** The bundled pure-JavaScript ssh2 1.17.0 negotiates with OpenSSH 7.4 on port 22 (MACs hmac-sha2-256, hmac-sha2-512, hmac-sha1), answers the keyboard-interactive prompts, and runs the install command that prints ASHLEY-KEY-INSTALLED. On a failure that is not an authentication failure, the wizard falls back to ssh in a terminal. It verifies no host key.
- **Check:** On the laptop, once the key works: `node dist/ashley-cli.js ssh2-probe` prints 'ssh2 reached warrior.grid.wayne.edu as <id>: warrior'. This proves negotiation with the same library; the password half needs a person.
- **Looks like:** `ssh2 failed: timed out`; `timed out talking to the grid`; `exec failed: ...`; `unexpected reply: ...`; `In-panel login did not work (...); falling back to a terminal.`; `Handshake failed: no matching ...`
- **Repair:** Fall back to the terminal login (existing behaviour).
- **By hand:** Use the terminal fallback and report the ssh2 error text.
- **Could break when:** ssh2 dropping SHA-1 MACs or older key-exchange methods; a login-node upgrade to algorithms ssh2 lacks; Node or Electron crypto changes that affect ed25519 key parsing.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:1-106,109-134; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:326-346; extension/src/cli/main.ts:145-150; extension/package.json:385

### `network.relay-session` The prompt relay session answers (READY and PONG)

Depends on: `grid-login.ashley-cli-installed`, `grid-login.noninteractive-shell-quiet`, `grid-login.ssh-session-limits`, `grid-login.long-lived-relay-allowed`

- **Assumes:** Each process holds one ssh session running `~/.ashley/bin/ashley relay`. The relay prints `READY <epoch ms> <version>`, answers PING with PONG in about 20 ms and SEND with QUEUED in about 40 ms (measured 2026-09-28), and pushes EVENT and RESULT lines. ServerAliveInterval=30 (with the block's CountMax of 5) detects a dead path. After 'close' the next call reconnects, and every GridApi call falls back to a fresh ssh on error. The READY version is parsed but never compared with the grid version bundled in the extension. The relay does not strip CR, so input sent with CRLF line endings is not understood.
- **Check:** On the laptop, sending LF-only input: from Node (spawn ssh with stdin 'PING\nQUIT\n'), or from Git Bash `printf 'PING\nQUIT\n' | ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid '~/.ashley/bin/ashley relay'`. Pass = 'READY <13 digits> <v>' then 'PONG <13 digits>' within 5 s, with <v> equal to the extension's dist/grid/VERSION. Alternatively `node dist/ashley-cli.js latency 1` shows ping_relay under 500 ms. Do not use a PowerShell pipe: it sends CRLF, and the relay then answers 'ERROR unknown command'. The grid-side counterpart is the node grid.relay in `ashley diag --json`, which runs the relay locally rather than over ssh.
- **Looks like:** `relay closed (ssh exit 1) ashley: unknown command 'relay' (ashley help)`; `relay closed (ssh exit 255)`; `relay is not connected`; `no QUEUED ack from the relay within 30 s`; `ERROR bad request or unknown worker '<w>'`; `ERROR unknown command (CRLF input)`; `bad result line: ...`; `ashley_result takes 10 s for an unfinished request: relay.ts ignores the grid's 'PENDING <id>' reply and waits for its 10 s timer`; `an ERROR line is charged to the newest waiter, because the ERROR reply carries no id`
- **Repair:** Call relay.close() and then ensure(). Safe. On a version mismatch or 'unknown command relay', upload the grid scripts again with the gridInstall step (scp plus `ashley install`, idempotent).
- **By hand:** Run Ashley: Install or update Ashley on the grid, then reload the window.
- **Could break when:** Protocol changes without any version negotiation. After laptop sleep, a half-open session can linger for up to ServerAliveInterval times CountMax.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/relay.ts:35-71,90-141,166-205; grid/ashley:389-434,759; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:125-175; extension/src/cli/main.ts:116-143; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:127-148

### `network.proxyjump-to-nodes` ProxyJump through the login node to compute nodes

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** 'Host *.nas.grid.wayne.edu' uses ProxyJump wsu-grid. This requires that warrior allows TCP forwarding (direct-tcpip), that warrior resolves node names (the short name from squeue %N plus nodeDomain), and that Windows OpenSSH can start its own ssh.exe for the jump. The laptop never reaches a node directly.
- **Check:** On the laptop: spawn `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid -W asx1.nas.grid.wayne.edu:22` (any node; use the worker's node when there is one) with stdin closed, and read the first line within 30 s. Pass = it starts with 'SSH-2.0-'. This proves forwarding, DNS on warrior and a listening node sshd, before pam_slurm_adopt is involved. On the login node, `getent hosts <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu` must resolve.
- **Looks like:** `channel 0: open failed: administratively prohibited: open failed`; `stdio forwarding failed`; `channel 0: open failed: connect failed: Name or service not known`; `CreateProcessW failed error:2`; `posix_spawnp: No such file or directory`; `Could not resolve hostname asx1: No such host is known. (a short name does not match the *.nas pattern)`
- **Repair:** Rewrite the managed block with stepSshConfig.run(). If Windows cannot start ssh for ProxyJump, write the equivalent 'ProxyCommand <absolute path to ssh.exe> -W %h:%p wsu-grid' inside the managed block. Same meaning, and reversible.
- **By hand:** Rerun the SSH configuration step. If C&IT has disabled forwarding, Remote-SSH to nodes cannot work; escalate.
- **Could break when:** AllowTcpForwarding disabled on login nodes (a common hardening step); the node domain renamed (the default nas.grid.wayne.edu is in store.ts:29 and cli/main.ts:42); Remote-SSH using a different ssh through remote.SSH.path.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:23-25,48-59,100-109; extension/src/extension.ts:232-283; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:29; extension/src/cli/main.ts:42

### `network.node-sshd-latency` Node ssh answers within Remote-SSH's timeout

Depends on: `grid-slurm.pam-slurm-adopt`

- **Assumes:** A compute node's sshd takes about 23 s before it runs anything; 62 s and 101 s were observed later, also from warrior and with bash --norc. The first Remote-SSH connection also downloads the VS Code server. commandConnect raises remote.SSH.connectTimeout to 300 s. The diagnostics engine's default per-check timeout of 90 s is too short for this node: give it about 320 s and mark it slow.
- **Check:** On the laptop (slow): time `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=300 <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu true`. Pass = under 300 s; log the time (baseline 23 to 101 s). The VS Code setting remote.SSH.connectTimeout must be 300 or more.
- **Looks like:** `Connecting with SSH timed out`; `Could not establish connection to "<node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu": The connection timed out`; `a second window timed out twice at 302 s while an earlier VS Code server was still running on the node (not diagnosed)`
- **Repair:** Set remote.SSH.connectTimeout to 300; connect already does this. For a stale server, `ssh <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu pkill -u $USER -f vscode-server` kills only the student's own processes; ask first.
- **By hand:** Wait and retry. Use 'Remote-SSH: Kill VS Code Server on Host', and read the newest 'Remote - SSH' output log.
- **Could break when:** Changes to node sshd or PAM (slower or faster); the VS Code server download (update.code.visualstudio.com) being blocked from nodes.
- **Code:** extension/src/extension.ts:268-271; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:139,246-253; docs/manual/90_troubleshooting.md

### `network.node-hostkeys` Compute-node host keys learnt and stable

Depends on: `network.proxyjump-to-nodes`

- **Assumes:** Node host keys are learnt on first use (StrictHostKeyChecking accept-new in the *.nas block) and do not change between visits.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `ssh-keygen -F <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu` finds an entry after the first connection, and its fingerprint equals the one the login node sees: `ssh wsu-grid ssh-keyscan -t ed25519,ecdsa,rsa <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu 2>/dev/null | ssh-keygen -lf -`.
- **Looks like:** `WARNING: REMOTE HOST IDENTIFICATION HAS CHANGED!`; `Host key verification failed.`
- **Repair:** When the key fetched through the authenticated login node differs from known_hosts: `ssh-keygen -R <node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu`, then append the keyscan line. The trust path is the already-authenticated jump host; log what changed.
- **By hand:** Do the same by hand after confirming the node was reimaged.
- **Could break when:** Reimaging nodes during an OS migration changes every node key at once.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:48-59

### `network.laptop-https-egress` Laptop HTTPS to the token services

- **Assumes:** The extension host's global fetch reaches api.github.com, huggingface.co, api.anthropic.com and ondemand.grid.wayne.edu with a trusted certificate chain. When it cannot (a proxy, TLS inspection, blocked hosts), validateToken() returns network:true, and the token is kept and verified on the grid instead.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `curl.exe -s -o NUL -m 10 -w "%{http_code} %{ssl_verify_result}" https://api.github.com/zen` returns '200 0'. https://huggingface.co/api/whoami-v2 and https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models without credentials return 401. Repeat from the extension host, because fetch treats VS Code's proxy settings differently from curl.
- **Looks like:** `this laptop's network refused the check (fetch failed)`; `self-signed certificate in certificate chain`; `unable to get local issuer certificate`; `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND api.github.com`; `stored (could not check it online just now)`
- **Repair:** None. Verification moves to the grid (grid-login.verify-tokens), which is the designed fallback.
- **By hand:** Try another network or the VPN. If a proxy is mandatory, set http.proxy in VS Code.
- **Could break when:** VS Code changing proxy support for fetch in the extension host; TLS inspection on campus or hospital networks.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:361-382; extension/src/core/services.ts:38,52,66,80; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:98-113; docs/manual/00_getting_started.md

### `network.compute-node-egress` Compute nodes reach the internet services Claude needs

Depends on: `grid-slurm.worker-running`

- **Assumes:** From inside jobs, nodes reach api.anthropic.com (Claude), registry.npmjs.org (the Claude Code install), registry-1.docker.io (the sandbox build), github.com and huggingface.co. Nothing inbound is needed; the laptop never reaches a node.
- **Check:** On a compute node only, inside a running worker allocation, and slow: `srun --jobid <worker job> --overlap -n1 curl -s -m 8 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code} ' https://api.anthropic.com/ https://registry.npmjs.org/ https://github.com https://huggingface.co`. Pass = no 000. The bootstrap smoke line already records net=<code>, and `ashley diag --node` checks api.anthropic.com as node.probe.
- **Looks like:** `net=000 in the bootstrap log`; `STOP: npm install failed`; `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND api.anthropic.com`; `Connection error (in a worker result)`; `TLS handshake timeout (docker pull)`
- **Repair:** If one node fails and others work, resubmit the worker (safe). Otherwise nothing.
- **By hand:** Report to hpc@wayne.edu, naming the node.
- **Could break when:** Nodes placed behind an HTTP proxy (HTTPS_PROXY would then have to reach the container); Docker Hub limits on anonymous pulls.
- **Code:** grid/bootstrap.sbatch:90,95; grid/ashley:781-788

### `network.tcp22-banner` TCP 22 to the login node, sshd banner

Depends on: `network.grid-dns`

- **Assumes:** The laptop's network lets outbound TCP 22 reach warrior (hospital/DMC or guest Wi-Fi often blocks it), and sshd announces itself. The banner version is recorded so that an sshd upgrade shows up.
- **Check:** laptop: open TCP to warrior.grid.wayne.edu:22 (IPv4) and read the first line within 10 s. Pass = starts with 'SSH-2.0-'. Warn when it changes from the recorded 'OpenSSH_7.4'.
- **Looks like:** `connect ETIMEDOUT`; `ECONNREFUSED`; `kex_exchange_identification: Connection closed by remote host`
- **By hand:** Change networks (campus Wi-Fi, home, phone hotspot) or connect the WSU VPN.
- **Could break when:** C&IT restricting SSH to VPN or campus only. An sshd upgrade changing algorithms (re-verify MACs and host keys).
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:147-151; extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:34-47

### `network.grid-dns` Login host resolves to IPv4

Depends on: `laptop.os`

- **Assumes:** warrior.grid.wayne.edu (ashley.loginHost) has an A record. The block forces AddressFamily inet.
- **Check:** laptop: dns.lookup(loginHost, {family:4}). Pass = an address.
- **Looks like:** `ssh: Could not resolve hostname warrior.grid.wayne.edu`; `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`
- **By hand:** Check DNS or VPN. If the login host was renamed, set ashley.loginHost and rerun the SSH configuration step.
- **Could break when:** Login node rename or retirement (for example a new cluster name).
- **Code:** extension/package.json:301-304; extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:43

### `network.ssh-rate-limit` sshd connection limits not tripped

Depends on: `network.tcp22-banner`

- **Assumes:** Bursts of short ssh connections do not hit warrior's MaxStartups or a fail2ban-style ban: tree refresh, the hourly sweep, the relay, token checks, and every GridApi call without the relay.
- **Check:** laptop: three sequential `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` all succeed, and none prints kex_exchange_identification.
- **Looks like:** `kex_exchange_identification: read: Connection reset by peer`; `ssh_exchange_identification: Connection closed by remote host`; `Connection timed out during banner exchange`
- **Repair:** Back off: pause sweeps for 15 minutes and route calls through the relay.
- **By hand:** Wait. If it persists, ask hpc@wayne.edu whether your IP is blocked.
- **Could break when:** C&IT tightening MaxStartups or adding IP bans after failed logins.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/tree.ts:243,256-305; extension/src/vscode/diagnosticsUi.ts:35-39

### `network.ondemand-reachable` OnDemand reachable (2FA setup)

Depends on: `network.internet`

- **Assumes:** ondemand.grid.wayne.edu answers only from campus or the WSU VPN, and the wizard uses that as its VPN test. It matters only while 2FA is being set up.
- **Check:** laptop: GET https://ondemand.grid.wayne.edu/ with redirect manual within 8 s. Skip when twoFactorDone.
- **Looks like:** `fetch failed`; `ETIMEDOUT`
- **By hand:** Join the campus Wi-Fi or connect GlobalProtect VPN.
- **Could break when:** OnDemand URL change, or OnDemand opened to the internet (then the VPN inference is wrong).
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:88-105,231-266

### `network.grid-2fa-prompt-contract` Grid password + OTP prompt shape

Depends on: `laptop.ssh2-js-client`

- **Assumes:** warrior's keyboard-interactive auth asks for 'Password:' first and then a verification code (Google Authenticator PAM). The panel routes prompts by /pass/i and /verification|code|token/i.
- **Check:** during installKey only: log the prompt texts. Pass = the first matches /pass/i and a later one matches /code|verification/i. Skip once the key works.
- **Looks like:** `the grid rejected the password or the code (3x)`; `unexpected prompt text (Duo push, 'Passcode or option')`
- **By hand:** Use the terminal fallback and answer the prompts as shown.
- **Could break when:** WSU moving the grid to Duo or Microsoft MFA (push or number matching), which changes the prompts.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:55-80; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:161-169

### `network.node-proxyjump` ssh to a compute node through the jump host

Depends on: `network.grid-login`, `grid.worker`, `laptop.known-hosts-nodes`

- **Assumes:** ProxyJump via warrior needs AllowTcpForwarding on the login sshd. The node lets the student in only while they hold a running job there (pam_slurm_adopt). The node's sshd takes ~25 s, under remote.SSH.connectTimeout.
- **Check:** laptop (slow): `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=120 <worker node>.nas.grid.wayne.edu hostname`. Pass = the node name. Warn when it takes over 120 s.
- **Looks like:** `channel 0: open failed: administratively prohibited: open failed`; `Access denied by pam_slurm_adopt: you have no active jobs on this node`; `Connection closed by UNKNOWN port 65535`; `Connecting with SSH timed out`
- **Repair:** Start or restart a worker so a job holds the node.
- **By hand:** Start a worker first. If forwarding is prohibited, contact hpc@wayne.edu (Remote-SSH to nodes depends on it).
- **Could break when:** C&IT disabling TCP forwarding on the login node, changing the node domain, or making node sshd slower.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshConfig.ts:48-60; extension/src/extension.ts:215-266

## Accounts (30)

### `account.wsu-access-id` The student's Wayne State AccessID

Depends on: `vscode.auth-microsoft`

- **Assumes:** The AccessID matches /^[a-z]{2}\d{4}$/ and is the grid username. It comes from the Microsoft sign-in (the local part of AccessID@wayne.edu; subdomains allowed) or is typed by the student. It names the grid home and the ssh User. By lab convention, unverified, it also names the student's Claude and GitHub accounts.
- **Check:** Laptop: normaliseAccessId(settings.accessId) is not null. After the key is installed, `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'id -un; echo $HOME'` prints the accessId and gridHome(accessId).
- **Looks like:** `An AccessID looks like ab1234 (two letters, four digits).`; `<email> is not a Wayne State account; sign in with AccessID@wayne.edu`; `grid home differs from /wsu/home/<ab>/<ab12>/<id>`; `not signed in`
- **Repair:** If ashley.wsuEmail is set and its local part matches the pattern, copy it into ashley.accessId. Safe.
- **By hand:** Run 'Sign in with Wayne State' again, or type the AccessID when asked.
- **Could break when:** WSU issuing AccessIDs in another format: the regex in services.ts:88, tools/allow.py:15 and the admin grant input in extension.ts would reject them. If UPNs became firstname.lastname@wayne.edu, the AccessID could no longer be derived from the sign-in and the wizard would fall back to asking.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:88-98; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:167-214; tools/allow.py:15; extension/src/extension.ts:492-529

### `account.lab-allowlist` AccessID is on the lab's pre-approved list

Depends on: `vscode.bundle-integrity`, `network.https-egress`, `account.wsu-access-id`

- **Assumes:** The AccessID is either in extension/media/allowlist.json, which ships in the VSIX (committed in a2e1f69; today it holds ['go2432']), or in the JSON at ashley.allowlistUrl. The URL is fetched with a 10 s timeout and the bundled list is the fallback. A missing or unreadable list means no restriction. The check runs only on the laptop and is not a security boundary.
- **Check:** On the laptop: read <extensionPath>/media/allowlist.json (CLI: dist/../media/allowlist.json or dist/allowlist.json) and test accessIds.includes(id). When ashley.allowlistUrl is set, `curl -s -m 10 <url>` must return JSON with an accessIds array. Report which list decided.
- **Looks like:** `<id> is not on the lab's pre-approved list; ask your lead to add you`; `<id> is not on the lab's pre-approved list; ask your lead to add you, then sign in again`
- **Repair:** Re-fetch when ashley.allowlistUrl is set (existing).
- **By hand:** The lead runs `python tools/allow.py add <id>`, rebuilds the VSIX (`cd extension && npm run package`) and sends it, or updates the JSON behind ashley.allowlistUrl.
- **Could break when:** The host behind allowlistUrl moving. A VSIX built from a checkout without the file silently admits everyone.
- **Code:** extension/src/vscode/store.ts:97-120; extension/src/cli/main.ts:69-79; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:181-182,207-208; tools/allow.py:1-54; extension/media/allowlist.json

- **Check:** Laptop, indirect: the in-panel login is not refused on every attempt. After the key is installed, `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid id -un` succeeds.
- **Looks like:** `the grid rejected the password or the code (all three attempts)`; `Permission denied (publickey,keyboard-interactive).`
- **By hand:** Request an account at tech.wayne.edu/kb/high-performance-computing, naming the lead as sponsor (hpc@wayne.edu, 313-577-4743).

### `account.grid-password` WSU password valid, not expired, not locked

Depends on: `account.grid-hpc-account`

- **Assumes:** The password is valid, not expired, and the account is not locked. With key authentication, an expired password still blocks non-interactive logins through PAM.
- **Check:** Laptop, after the key is installed: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` exits 0 and stderr does not mention an expired password.
- **Looks like:** `WARNING: Your password has expired.`; `Password change required but no TTY available.`; `You are required to change your password immediately`; `Account locked`
- **By hand:** Change the password through WSU's account self-service, or wait out a lockout, then retry.
- **Could break when:** WSU password-policy changes.

Depends on: `account.grid-hpc-account`, `network.wsu-campus-or-vpn`

- **Assumes:** Google Authenticator is enrolled through OnDemand > Interactive Apps > 2FA Setup. The six-digit TOTP, from a phone with the correct time, is accepted at the verification-code prompt of password logins.
- **Check:** Human only. Ashley records ashley.twoFactorDone, and a successful installKey proves it.

### `account.grid-key-authorized` The grid accepts the laptop's key without prompting

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-key-pair`, `laptop.ssh-config-block`, `laptop.ssh-known-hosts-login`, `network.grid-login`, `network.sshd-algorithms`, `account.grid-password`

- **Assumes:** The public key is in the grid's ~/.ssh/authorized_keys with modes sshd accepts, so `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid hostname` answers with no prompt. A key alone is sufficient: the grid does not ask for a second factor after publickey.
- **Check:** Laptop: testConnection(alias) returns ok with a non-empty hostname (stepInstallKey.check).
- **Looks like:** `Permission denied (publickey,keyboard-interactive).`; `Authenticated using "publickey" with partial success.`; `Cannot reach the grid`
- **By hand:** Run the wizard step 'Log in to the grid once' (password and code, once).
- **Could break when:** If C&IT required publickey plus keyboard-interactive on every login, every BatchMode path would break at once: extension, relay, MCP and the Remote-SSH ProxyJump.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:147-151,316-359; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:27-28

### `account.github-token` GitHub token valid with the needed scopes

Depends on: `vscode.auth-github`, `external.github-api`, `vscode.secret-storage`

- **Assumes:** A GitHub token is on the laptop, either gho_ from the sign-in or a pasted ghp_ or github_pat_ token. GET /user returns 200 with a login, and the token can push to the lab's repositories. The paste pattern accepts only ghp_ and github_pat_, so a pasted `gh auth token` value (gho_) is refused.
- **Check:** On the laptop: validateToken(github) succeeds and returns the login; a network failure counts as WARN, and the grid then verifies. Scopes: X-OAuth-Scopes includes 'repo'. Fine-grained tokens send no such header, so for them `GET /repos/<owner>/<repo>` must show permissions.push true. On the grid: `ashley verify-tokens` prints 'GITHUB_TOKEN ok (<login>)' (diag id grid.token-github).
- **Looks like:** `no longer accepted (the service rejected it (HTTP 401)): press Renew`; `Resource protected by organization SAML enforcement`; `That does not look like the right kind of token.`; `GITHUB_TOKEN rejected or unreachable`; `remote: Permission to <owner>/<repo>.git denied to <login>.`
- **Repair:** Renew through VS Code's GitHub sign-in (one click); pushTokens() then updates the grid. The daily checkTokensQuietly() already offers this.
- **By hand:** Renew the token. For organisation repositories, ask an owner to approve VS Code's OAuth app, or create a fine-grained token with Contents: read and write.
- **Could break when:** Deprecation of classic PATs; organisation token-lifetime policies (fine-grained tokens pending approval, a 366-day default); prefix changes.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:28-43; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:361-382,384-451; extension/src/extension.ts:179-192; grid/ashley:797-801

### `account.huggingface-token` Hugging Face token present, accepted and writable

Depends on: `external.huggingface-api`, `vscode.secret-storage`

- **Assumes:** An hf_ token with the write role is on the laptop, and whoami-v2 returns 200 with a name. Neither the laptop nor the grid checks the role, so a read token passes validation and only fails later, on upload.
- **Check:** On the laptop: validateToken(huggingface) succeeds, and in the whoami-v2 JSON auth.accessToken.role is 'write', or 'fineGrained' with write access to the repositories. On the grid: 'HF_TOKEN ok (<name>)' (diag id grid.token-hf).
- **Looks like:** `no longer accepted (the service rejected it (HTTP 401)): press Renew`; `403 Forbidden on hf upload from the grid (read-only token)`; `the service rejected it (HTTP 401)`; `HF_TOKEN rejected or unreachable`; `403 Forbidden: You don't have the rights to create a model under this namespace`; `HTTP 429`
- **Repair:** On a 429 or a timeout, retry once after 60 s. Safe.
- **By hand:** Create a Write token on the prefilled page (name wsu-grid) and copy it; the panel picks it up.
- **Could break when:** Hugging Face changing token types, the rate limits on whoami, or the query parameters of the token page.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:44-57; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:361-382; grid/ashley:793-797

### `account.claude-subscription-token` Claude subscription token valid (setup-token, one year)

Depends on: `vscode.claude-setup-token`, `external.anthropic-oauth-probe`

- **Assumes:** The student has Claude Pro or Max (assumed to be the AccessID@wayne.edu account) and a setup-token OAuth token valid for one year. It is not refreshed after a 401; it must be regenerated.
- **Check:** Laptop: validateToken(claudeOauth) returns ok. Record the date the token was stored in globalState and warn at 330 days.
- **Looks like:** `OAuth access token is invalid`; `no longer accepted (the service rejected it (HTTP 401)): press Renew`; `worker results with status rate_limited`
- **Repair:** Renew, which runs setup-token again.
- **By hand:** Press Renew next to Claude, sign in on claude.ai and press Authorize.
- **Could break when:** Anthropic changing subscription-token policy for automation, the token lifetime or plan eligibility.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:58-71; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:412-428; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:95-115

### `account.grid-authorized-key` This laptop's key is authorized on the grid

Depends on: `laptop.ssh-key`, `grid-login.sshd-publickey-sufficient`

- **Assumes:** The public key ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu.pub (comment '<AccessID>@wayne.edu ashley') is a line of ~/.ssh/authorized_keys in the grid home. sshd StrictModes must accept the permissions: home not writable by group or others, ~/.ssh 700, authorized_keys 600. With IdentitiesOnly, only this key is offered.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o PreferredAuthentications=publickey wsu-grid true` exits 0. A password-expiry message in stderr also means the key was accepted (see account.wsu-password-current). Once a session works: `ssh wsu-grid "grep -c ashley ~/.ssh/authorized_keys; stat -c '%a %U' ~ ~/.ssh ~/.ssh/authorized_keys"` must show a count of 1 or more, modes no looser than 755, 700 and 600, and the AccessID as owner. The fingerprint from `ssh-keygen -lf ~/.ssh/id_ed25519_wsu.pub` must appear in the output of `ssh wsu-grid ssh-keygen -lf ~/.ssh/authorized_keys`.
- **Looks like:** `Permission denied (publickey,keyboard-interactive).`; `Permission denied (publickey,keyboard-interactive,password).`; `unexpected reply: ... (the in-panel install did not print ASHLEY-KEY-INSTALLED)`; `the grid does not accept the key yet`
- **Repair:** Only with a working session: `chmod go-w ~; chmod 700 ~/.ssh; chmod 600 ~/.ssh/authorized_keys` (the student's own files). Without a session, the repair is the wizard step 'Log in to the grid once', which needs the password and the Authenticator code from a person.
- **By hand:** Rerun 'Log in to the grid once'. If it reports success but key logins still fail, open a shell through OnDemand and run the chmod line above.
- **Could break when:** C&IT moving keys to a central store (AuthorizedKeysCommand) or forbidding user-managed keys. FIPS mode on a RHEL 9 login node would reject ed25519 keys; an ECDSA P-256 or RSA 3072 key would then be needed.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:27-28; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:274-292,316-358; docs/manual/90_troubleshooting.md

### `account.wsu-password-current` AccessID password not expired

Depends on: `account.grid-authorized-key`

- **Assumes:** sshd runs the PAM account phase even for key logins. An expired AccessID password forces a change, which a non-interactive session cannot perform, so key logins break although the key itself is fine.
- **Check:** On the laptop: the stderr of `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` contains neither 'password has expired' nor 'Password change required'. Pass = neither text present.
- **Looks like:** `WARNING: Your password has expired.`; `Password change required but no TTY available.`; `You are required to change your password immediately (password aged)`
- **By hand:** Change the AccessID password through Wayne State's password service (C&IT), then retry. An interactive `ssh wsu-grid` may also offer the change.
- **Could break when:** WSU changing password rotation, or a directory migration (AD or Entra) that changes how expiry is enforced.
- **Code:** No explicit code; implicit in extension/src/core/wizard.ts:147-151 and extension/src/core/shell.ts:96 (BatchMode everywhere)

### `account.grid-home-layout` Grid home is /wsu/home/ab/ab12/ab1234

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** gridHome() builds /wsu/home/<first 2 characters>/<first 4 characters>/<AccessID>. The result becomes ASHLEY_GRID_HOME in local terminals and the default folder that commandConnect opens in Remote-SSH.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'id -un; echo $HOME'`. Pass = the first line equals ashley.accessId and the second equals gridHome(accessId).
- **Looks like:** `the Remote-SSH window opens an empty or non-existent folder`; `ASHLEY_GRID_HOME points to a path that does not exist`
- **Repair:** Use the $HOME the grid reports instead of the computed path. This is safe (read only) but needs a small code change wherever services.ts gridHome() is called.
- **By hand:** Type the correct folder when Ashley asks which folder to open.
- **Could break when:** C&IT reorganising home directories or moving homes off Panasas.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:95-107; extension/src/extension.ts:232-283; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:62-86

Depends on: `vscode.microsoft-auth-provider`

- **Assumes:** WSU's Microsoft Entra tenant allows students to consent to VS Code's first-party app for User.Read, no Conditional Access policy blocks the sign-in, and the account's MFA completes in the browser.

### `account.grid-account-active` Grid account exists and is active

Depends on: `account.wsu-access-id`

- **Assumes:** An HPC account exists for the AccessID and is neither expired nor disabled. It is requested at tech.wayne.edu naming the sponsor and takes about two business days.
- **Check:** If the key already works: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'id -un'` returns the AccessID (pass). Otherwise the laptop cannot tell, because sshd does not reveal unknown users, so return WARN 'unverified; log in at ondemand.grid.wayne.edu to prove it'. The lab admin can check from the login node with `getent passwd <id>`.

### `account.grid-totp-enrolled` Google Authenticator enrolled for the grid

Depends on: `account.grid-account-active`, `network.campus-vpn-ondemand`

- **Assumes:** The student enrolled Google Authenticator through OnDemand > Interactive Apps > 2FA Setup (a QR code). The grid's keyboard-interactive login asks 'Password:' and then a verification code. The in-panel client routes prompts by regex: /pass/i goes to the password field (once) and /verification|code|token/i to the code field. The phone's clock and the grid's clock agree within the TOTP window. ashley.twoFactorDone is the student's own claim; only a successful login proves it.
- **Check:** Skip when the key already works. Otherwise a person does one login in 'Log in to the grid once'. Once a session exists, a cheap clock check: the difference between `ssh wsu-grid date +%s` and the laptop's NTP time is under 30 s (the relay's PONG also carries the grid clock).
- **Looks like:** `the grid rejected the password or the code`; `The grid rejected that password or code (attempt N of 3). Try again.`; `three rejected logins: check the account exists and redo the Authenticator setup (the previous step opens OnDemand), then run the wizard again`; `Permission denied (keyboard-interactive).`
- **Repair:** After three rejected logins, set ashley.twoFactorDone back to false and route the student back to 'Grid account and Authenticator'. This is already implemented.
- **By hand:** Redo 2FA Setup on OnDemand (campus Wi-Fi or VPN). On Android, turn on the Authenticator's time correction. Wait for a fresh code before typing it.
- **Could break when:** WSU replacing Google Authenticator with Duo or Microsoft Authenticator push. The prompt texts would change, a push approval cannot be typed into a field, and the wizard's links and instructions would be wrong. The OnDemand 2FA Setup app could also be renamed, or the KB article moved.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:217-272,327-339; extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:56-80; extension/src/vscode/store.ts:152-160; extension/src/cli/main.ts:81-89

### `account.claude-subscription` Claude Pro or Max subscription on the student's own account

- **Assumes:** The student holds Claude Pro or Max; setup-token is issued only for Pro, Max, Team or Enterprise. By lab convention the account was made with AccessID@wayne.edu, but nothing verifies the e-mail: the admin queue says 'Claude account (assumed) <id>@wayne.edu'. One person, one token.
- **Check:** Ground truth that does not depend on the validator: a trivial worker request (`ashley ask`, mode plan, max_turns 1) returns status ok with account 'default'. Optionally, GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage with the token and 'anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20' returns five_hour and seven_day windows (documented in RESEARCH.md, not used in the code). Otherwise report WARN 'unverified'.
- **Looks like:** `no enabled Claude account`; `rate_limited with retry_after soon after the worker starts`; `setup-token refuses a free plan (the exact message was not recorded)`
- **By hand:** Subscribe to Claude Pro (the minimum) with the WSU e-mail account, then run the Claude step again.
- **Could break when:** Plan changes (setup-token limited to higher tiers); changes to the Consumer Terms on automated use of a subscription.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:58-71; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:412-428; extension/src/extension.ts:492-529; docs/RESEARCH.md:47,79,142

### `account.claude-oauth-token` Claude subscription token accepted on the laptop and the grid

Depends on: `vscode.secret-storage`, `account.claude-subscription`

- **Assumes:** The sk-ant-oat token counts as valid when GET /v1/models with Bearer, 'anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20' and anthropic-version returns 200. NOT KNOWN: no 200 has been recorded for a real setup-token, and the access review asked for one. The laptop refuses the token on 401 or 403. The grid passes it only on 200, and `access requirements` blocks on anything else. So if this endpoint answers valid subscription tokens with 403 or 400, every student is refused at the last step and the laptop rejects good tokens. The grid copy is ~/.ashley/accounts/default/token (mode 600). The token lasts one year, and revocation has been reported to take 3 to 4 days.
- **Check:** On the laptop, validateToken(claudeOauth) succeeds. On the grid, `ashley verify-tokens | grep ^CLAUDE_TOKEN` prints 'CLAUDE_TOKEN ok (subscription token accepted)' (diag id grid.token-claude). Independent ground truth: a trivial worker request returns status ok without 'Failed to authenticate'. Record all three results. If the validator says no and the worker run says yes, the validator is wrong.
- **Looks like:** `CLAUDE_TOKEN rejected (invalid, expired or revoked: run claude setup-token again)`; `CLAUDE_TOKEN present (the check answered HTTP <code>)`; `the Claude token could not be confirmed right now (...); try again in a minute`; `OAuth access token is invalid`; `Failed to authenticate`; `the service rejected it (HTTP 403)`
- **Repair:** Nothing for an invalid token. When the validator and the worker disagree, trust the worker run and flag services.ts:66 and grid/ashley:807 for an update.
- **By hand:** Run the Claude step again (Authorize in the browser), then Install on the grid to push the new token.
- **Could break when:** High: the beta header retired, /v1/models restricted for OAuth tokens, and the yearly expiry (the token must be renewed every year).
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:58-71; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:361-382,471-490; grid/ashley:575-590,803-813; docs/research/access_review_2026-09-28.json:245-251; docs/RESEARCH.md:47-48,146

### `account.anthropic-api-key` Optional Anthropic API key (overflow)

> Since 5.0.0 (2026-10-04) there is no API key: none is asked for, stored, copied or used. A key 4.0.0 stored is never used or sent; on the grid an empty `disabled` file is put beside it, the switch a worker still on grid scripts 7 honours, and `grid.accounts` reports it until `beethoven accounts remove --yes` deletes it; on the laptop Manage stored tokens or Reset deletes it. The entry below describes 4.0.0, except the step by hand, which is today's.

Depends on: `vscode.secret-storage`

- **Assumes:** An optional sk-ant-api key. Since wizard v2 it is no longer a wizard step; it is set through Manage tokens. GET /v1/models with X-Api-Key returns 200. On the grid it is stored at ~/.ashley/accounts/apikey/apikey (mode 600). It is never injected into laptop terminals, and the grid's verify-tokens does not check it.
- **Check:** Skip when not set. On the laptop, validateToken(anthropicApiKey) succeeds. On the grid, `stat -c %a ~/.ashley/accounts/apikey/apikey` prints 600, and `ashley status --json` lists 'apikey' among the accounts.
- **Looks like:** `the service rejected it (HTTP 401)`; `invalid x-api-key`; `credit balance is too low`
- **By hand:** Nothing to set up: Beethoven uses no API key. To delete a key 4.0.0 stored: Manage stored tokens or Reset on the laptop, beethoven accounts remove --yes in a terminal on the grid; revoke the key itself in the Anthropic Console.
- **Could break when:** Changes to the Console URL (console.anthropic.com may redirect) or to billing policy.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:72-85; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:485-488; extension/src/extension.ts:424-439

### `account.lab-access-approval` Lab access granted (shared folder readable)

Depends on: `grid-login.verify-tokens`, `grid-slurm.access-request-visibility`

- **Assumes:** Approval means named-user POSIX ACLs on /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared, set by its owner, the lab admin. The request is refused until every wizard step is done and the Claude, GitHub and Hugging Face tokens verify on the grid. The unix group oschome lives in the university directory and changes only when C&IT acts on the admin's e-mail.
- **Check:** On the laptop, `ssh wsu-grid "~/.ashley/bin/ashley access whoami --json"` shows admin true, or state 'approved' with granted true (diag id grid.lab-access). On the login node, `test -r /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley/library.json && echo ok` prints ok. Log `getent group oschome`.
- **Looks like:** `Waiting for lab access`; `denied: <message>; ask your lead to reset your request to ask again`; `approved, but the lab folder is not readable: the admin should run ashley access admin resync`; `not yet: <missing items>`; `finish the setup first; requests are for working environments`
- **Repair:** When access is granted but the container has not been adopted, run `ashley install`, which adopts the lab container in a second. Safe.

### `account.grid-login-shell` Grid login shell is bash

Depends on: `account.wsu-access-id`

- **Assumes:** The AccessID's passwd shell is /bin/bash. Every laptop call is `ssh wsu-grid -- '<single-quoted argv>'`, and the remote login shell runs it: it expands the unquoted leading `~/`, and pushTokens and GridApi.sbatch run `bash -c '...'`. Because the shell is Red Hat bash, ~/.bashrc is sourced for these non-interactive commands, which puts ~/.ashley/bin and the VS Code sysroot variables in place.
- **Check:** laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'getent passwd "$USER" | cut -d: -f7; echo $BASH_VERSION'`. Pass = `/bin/bash` and a version >= 4.2.
- **Looks like:** `Illegal variable name.`; `Unmatched '.`; `bash: -c: line 0: unexpected EOF while looking for matching`; `Remote-SSH exit code 207 (bashrc block never sourced under tcsh/zsh)`
- **By hand:** Ask C&IT (hpc@wayne.edu) to set the login shell to /bin/bash. Ashley supports no other login shell.
- **Could break when:** Low. An identity-management migration could reset login shells to a site default.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:103-108; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:479-487; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:212-216; grid/ashley:1

### `account.slurm-association` Slurm association allows reqp/requeue, secp/secondary (and gpu)

Depends on: `account.wsu-access-id`

- **Assumes:** The account has a Slurm association and a default account that allow partition reqp with QoS requeue, and secp with QoS secondary. reqp/requeue is hard-coded in bootstrap.sbatch, as the worker default and on the held access-request job. GPU users also need QoS gpu on gmsap/gvohp/galhp.
- **Check:** login node: `sacctmgr -nP show assoc user=$USER format=account,partition,qos` and `sacctmgr -nP show user $USER format=defaultaccount`. Cheap proof: `sbatch --test-only -p reqp -q requeue -t 1 --mem=16M --wrap true 2>&1` prints 'to start at'. Run the same test for secp/secondary.
- **Looks like:** `sbatch: error: Batch job submission failed: Invalid account or account/partition combination specified`; `sbatch: error: Batch job submission failed: Invalid qos specification`; `Job's QOS not permitted to use this partition`; `ashley: could not file the request: sbatch: error:`; `ashley: sbatch failed:`
- **Repair:** The held access-request job never runs. If reqp/requeue refuses it, resubmit it on secp/secondary. No other repair.
- **By hand:** The student emails hpc@wayne.edu, naming their sponsor, to ask for reqp/requeue and secp/secondary (and gpu if needed).
- **Could break when:** C&IT renames or consolidates QoS (requeue/secondary/primary/gpu) or partitions, or moves to per-lab accounts instead of per-user defaults. Every hard-coded pair then fails at submit.
- **Code:** grid/bootstrap.sbatch:8-10; grid/ashley:75, 247, 599; extension/src/core/slurm.ts:59-75

> Since 5.0.0 there is no rotation and no overflow onto an API key: a spent window is answered `rate_limited` with `retry_after`, and the request is sent again after the reset. No API key and no usage credits, by anyone: extra usage (usage credits) on the Claude account is the student's to keep off. The assumption, the repair and the step by hand below are today's; the study's text of 4.0.0 stays in the JSON record.

Depends on: `account.claude-grid-credential`

- **Repair:** None: the worker answers rate_limited with retry_after and starts nothing until the reset.
- **By hand:** Wait for the reset, then send the request again. No API key, no usage credits.

## Grid login node (54)

### `grid-login.ashley-cli` Ashley's grid CLI answers (as seen from the laptop)

Depends on: `account.grid-key-authorized`

- **Assumes:** ~/.ashley/bin/ashley exists and runs on the login node. The laptop's calls go through it: version, status --json, access, worker, relay, phone and verify-tokens. The grid subsystem owns the internals; this node is the laptop's view of it.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid '~/.ashley/bin/ashley version'` exits 0 and prints a version.
- **Looks like:** `bash: /wsu/home/.../.ashley/bin/ashley: No such file or directory`; `Ashley is not installed on the grid yet`
- **Repair:** Run stepGridInstall.run.
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Install or update Ashley on the grid'.
- **Could break when:** A full home quota. Panasas outages.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:464-469; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:37-53

### `grid-login.bashrc-block` The ~/.bashrc block exports the VS Code sysroot (as seen from the laptop)

Depends on: `grid-login.ashley-cli`, `grid-login.ashley-scripts-complete`

- **Assumes:** ~/.bashrc carries exactly one '# >>> ashley' ... '# <<< ashley' block, identical to ~/.ashley/bin/bashrc.block (the VS Code glibc sysroot variables plus PATH to ~/.ashley/bin). `ashley install` rewrites it idempotently by its markers, `ashley uninstall` removes it, and `access requirements` refuses to pass without it.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'grep -c ">>> ashley" ~/.bashrc; test -x /wsu/apps/vscode/patchelf/0.19.1/bin/patchelf && echo patchelf-ok'` prints a count >= 1 and patchelf-ok.
- **Looks like:** `found GLIBC 2.17`; `Exit code 207`; `The remote host may not meet VS Code Server's prerequisites`; `FAIL bashrc block missing: run ashley install`; `bashrc block missing (run the grid step again)`; `ashley: command not found (in a grid shell)`
- **Repair:** `ssh wsu-grid '~/.ashley/bin/ashley install'` rewrites the block by its markers.
- **By hand:** Run 'Ashley: Install or update Ashley on the grid'.
- **Could break when:** Once the grid runs EL8+, C&IT may retire the sysroot and the block would point at missing files, so it must be versioned out. If VS Code renames the variables, the block silently stops working.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:117-124, 232, 580, 664-665; grid/bashrc.block:1-9

### `grid-login.sshd-publickey-sufficient` The grid accepts the key alone (no second factor on key logins)

Depends on: `network.grid-login-hostkey`, `network.ssh-mac-hmac-sha1`, `network.ssh-ipv4-only`

- **Assumes:** sshd on warrior lets publickey authentication complete by itself; Google Authenticator is asked only on the password path. Everything after the one password-and-code login depends on this: BatchMode calls, the relay, the MCP bridge, Remote-SSH and ProxyJump.
- **Check:** On the laptop, run `ssh -v -o BatchMode=yes -o PreferredAuthentications=publickey wsu-grid true` and read stderr. FAIL if it contains 'partial success': sshd now requires a second factor after the key. PASS if it exits 0 after 'Authenticated to warrior.grid.wayne.edu ... using "publickey"'. WARN, not fail, if it says 'Permission denied' without 'partial success': the key is not accepted yet, so the policy cannot be judged.
- **Looks like:** `Authenticated using "publickey" with partial success.`; `Permission denied (keyboard-interactive).`; `Permission denied (publickey,keyboard-interactive).`
- **By hand:** If WSU now requires a second factor even with keys, Ashley's prompt-free design no longer holds. Escalate to Greg. The likely route is one long-lived ssh2 session per laptop that answers the second factor once, because Windows OpenSSH has no ControlMaster.
- **Could break when:** High. Policies that require MFA on every login (Duo, Microsoft MFA) are a common HPC hardening step, and they would break every non-interactive path at once.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/sshInstall.ts:1-5; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:254,316-358; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (Secrets table: password and code typed once)

### `grid-login.noninteractive-shell-quiet` Non-interactive ssh sessions print nothing

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** Red Hat's bash sources ~/.bashrc for commands run by sshd (SSH_SOURCE_BASHRC), so anything the student's startup files print to stdout arrives ahead of Ashley's output. The relay protocol, scp over SFTP, testConnection() (the first word of stdout is taken as the hostname) and parseLastJson() all need a clean stdout. The Ashley block itself prints nothing.
- **Check:** On the laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true` writes 0 bytes to stdout (PowerShell: `(ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid true | Out-String).Length -eq 0`; POSIX: `| wc -c` gives 0). To find the culprit: `ssh wsu-grid 'bash -x ~/.bashrc' 2>&1 | head -40`.
- **Looks like:** `Received message too long <n>`; `Ensure the remote shell produces no output for non-interactive sessions.`; `protocol error: ...`; `the Ashley view shows 'wsu-grid -> <text>' instead of warrior`; `status did not return JSON`
- **By hand:** Guard output in ~/.bashrc with `[[ $- == *i* ]] && echo ...`, or send it to stderr. Keep conda and module messages quiet.
- **Could break when:** On a RHEL 8 or 9 login node whose bash no longer sources ~/.bashrc for ssh commands, the Ashley block (the VS Code sysroot variables and PATH) would no longer apply to Remote-SSH sessions.
- **Code:** grid/bashrc.block:1-9; extension/src/core/relay.ts:44-55; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:147-151; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:240-253; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:255

### `grid-login.sftp-subsystem` Script upload by scp (SFTP mode) into ~/.ashley/bin

Depends on: `grid-login.noninteractive-shell-quiet`, `grid-login.sshd-pubkey-batchmode`, `grid-login.bashrc-quiet`

- **Assumes:** The scp in Windows OpenSSH 9.5 uses the SFTP protocol by default, so the grid's sshd must offer the sftp subsystem. '~/.ashley/bin/' works because scp in SFTP mode treats a leading ~/ as relative to the login directory, so OpenSSH 7.4 needs no expand-path extension. The grid install upload and push_via_grid.sh both use scp.
- **Check:** On the laptop, from Git Bash or with a batch file that has LF line endings: `printf 'pwd\n' | sftp -b - -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid` prints 'Remote working directory: /wsu/home/...' and exits 0.
- **Looks like:** `subsystem request failed on channel 0`; `scp: Connection closed`; `upload failed: ...`; `server expand-path extension is required for ~user paths in SFTP mode`; `Received message too long`; `upload failed:`
- **Repair:** Retry with `scp -O` (legacy protocol). If that also fails, stream each file through `ssh wsu-grid 'cat > ~/.ashley/bin/<f>'` on stdin and chmod afterwards.
- **By hand:** Rerun 'Install on the grid'. If sftp is disabled on the login node, ask hpc@wayne.edu, or copy with `ssh wsu-grid 'cat > file' < file`.
- **Could break when:** C&IT disabling sftp on login nodes and moving transfers to a data node; OpenSSH eventually removing scp -O.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/shell.ts:110-113; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:531-535; tools/push_via_grid.sh:13

### `grid-login.ssh-session-limits` Several ssh sessions per user are allowed at once

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** The login node tolerates several concurrent sessions per user: one relay per VS Code window, one per MCP server process (Claude Code starts one per session), one per CLI, plus short-lived calls and Remote-SSH jumps. No maxlogins or MaxStartups limit is reached.
- **Check:** On the login node (cheap): `pgrep -u $USER -fc 'ashley relay'`, `who | grep -c "^$USER "` and `grep -hs maxlogins /etc/security/limits.conf /etc/security/limits.d/*`. Pass = fewer than 10 relays and fewer sessions than any maxlogins value.
- **Looks like:** `Too many logins for '<id>'.`; `kex_exchange_identification: read: Connection reset by peer`; `ssh_exchange_identification: Connection closed by remote host`; `relay closed (ssh exit 255), repeatedly`
- **Repair:** Kill only this user's stale relays on the login node with `pkill -u $USER -f 'ashley relay'`; clients reconnect on their next call. Safe.
- **By hand:** Close unused VS Code windows and Claude Code sessions.
- **Could break when:** Per-user session caps or idle-session reapers on the login node.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/relay.ts:25-71; extension/src/extension.ts:25-32; extension/src/mcp/server.ts:18-23; extension/src/cli/main.ts:101

### `grid-login.long-lived-relay-allowed` A relay process may run for hours on the login node

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** `ashley relay` is a bash `read -t 0.25` loop that uses almost no CPU, and it is allowed to run for hours on warrior. C&IT kills VS Code servers and heavy processes there, but not idle shells. The relay needs bash 4 or newer (associative arrays, fractional read timeout) and GNU date (%3N).
- **Check:** On the login node: `bash --version | head -1` shows 4 or newer, and `date +%s%3N` prints 13 digits. If a relay is running, `ps -o etimes=,pcpu= -p $(pgrep -u $USER -f 'ashley relay' | head -1)` shows elapsed seconds past 3600 at about 0 % CPU. WARN if no relay exists to observe.
- **Looks like:** `relay closed (ssh exit 255)`; `relay closed (ssh exit null) Killed`; `local: -A: invalid option`; `relay send failed (...); using a fresh ssh (repeated in the Ashley log)`
- **Repair:** Nothing is needed for correctness: GridApi falls back to a fresh ssh per call, at about 2 s instead of 40 ms.
- **By hand:** Ask C&IT whether an idle-process policy applies to the login node.
- **Could break when:** A process watchdog on the login node extended to long-lived processes; a restricted shell.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:389-434; extension/src/core/relay.ts:35-71; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:125-175

### `grid-login.tokens-pushed` Tokens copied to the grid (names match)

Depends on: `network.grid-login`, `grid-login.ashley-cli-installed`

- **Assumes:** pushTokens() writes ~/.ashley/secrets.env (GITHUB_TOKEN, GH_TOKEN, HF_TOKEN; the whole file is overwritten each time), ~/.ashley/accounts/default/token and accounts/apikey/apikey. It sends them over ssh stdin with umask 077, never on the command line. `ashley status --json` lists the NAMES of the secrets and accounts, so the laptop can compare.
- **Check:** On the laptop: in the output of `ssh wsu-grid "~/.ashley/bin/ashley status --json"`, .secrets contains GITHUB_TOKEN and HF_TOKEN whenever the laptop has those tokens, and .accounts contains 'default' (oauth-token). On the login node: `stat -c %a ~/.ashley/secrets.env ~/.ashley/accounts/default/token` prints 600 600.
- **Looks like:** `grid is missing: GitHub token, Hugging Face token, Claude token`; `writing secrets.env: ...`; `writing the Claude token: ...`; `; the grid copy was not updated (...)`; `secrets.env missing (GitHub / Hugging Face tokens for the worker)`
- **Repair:** Run pushTokens() again. It is idempotent and uses stdin only, so it is safe.
- **By hand:** Run Ashley: Install or update Ashley on the grid.
- **Could break when:** Because the whole file is overwritten, any other variable a student or lead adds to secrets.env (for example an NCBI key) is lost. A full home quota makes the write fail.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:471-490,500-526; grid/ashley:189-194

### `grid-login.https-egress-and-ca` Login node HTTPS egress and CA trust

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** The login node reaches api.github.com, huggingface.co, api.anthropic.com and github.com over HTTPS, and CentOS 7's curl (7.29, built on NSS) trusts their current certificate chains through its ca-certificates bundle. CentOS 7 is past end of life, so that trust store only gets older.
- **Check:** On the login node: `curl -s -m 10 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://api.github.com/zen https://huggingface.co/api/whoami-v2 https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models` prints 200, 401, 401, never 000. Log `curl --version | head -1` and `rpm -q ca-certificates`. The corresponding diag id is grid.internet.
- **Looks like:** `000`; `Peer's Certificate issuer is not recognized.`; `SSL connect error`; `Could not resolve host`; `HF_TOKEN rejected or unreachable while the laptop verifies the same token`; `CLAUDE_TOKEN present (the check answered HTTP 000)`
- **Repair:** Verify through an independent path instead: run the same curl inside the worker container, which has Debian 12's CA store, with `srun --jobid <worker> --overlap -n1`. This is cheap, runs on the compute node and is safe.
- **By hand:** Tell hpc@wayne.edu that the ca-certificates or egress need attention. Meanwhile rely on the laptop's verification.
- **Could break when:** High. A CA root rotation by any of the three vendors breaks an unpatched CentOS 7 trust store. C&IT could also close egress from the login node. An OS migration would fix both.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:506-513,708-711,789-813

### `grid-login.curl-config-stdin` curl reads secret headers from stdin (-K -)

Depends on: `network.grid-login`

- **Assumes:** `curl -K -` reads 'header = "..."' and 'url = "..."' lines from stdin, so tokens never appear in the login node's process list; three verify runs were sampled and none was visible. The -w '%{http_code}' and -m options work.
- **Check:** On the login node: `printf 'header = "X-Ashley-Diag: 1"\n' | curl -s -m 10 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' -K - https://api.github.com/zen` prints 200. While verify-tokens runs, `ps -u $USER -o args | grep -c Bearer` counts 0.
- **Looks like:** `000 or an empty status code`; `a curl warning about the config read from stdin`; `a token visible in ps output`
- **Repair:** None. Never fall back to passing the token on the command line.
