### `container.claude-limit-message-contract` Rate-limit text and reset time parse

Depends on: `container.claude-headless-contract`

- **Assumes:** Limit exhaustion shows up in result or stderr text that matches RATE_LIMIT_PATTERNS, with 'resets <h>[:mm]am/pm' or 'try again in N unit'. The reset hour is read in the container's local time zone, which comes from the host's /etc/localtime bound by Apptainer and must match the zone Claude prints. overloaded_error is treated as a limit, which cools the account for an hour.
- **Check:** grid (in the worker): `date +%Z` inside the container equals the host's (EDT/EST). Unit test parseResetTime on the latest real limit message saved in accounts/*/state.json last_error.
- **Looks like:** `cooldown_until off by 4-5 h`; `rate_limited during an Anthropic outage (overloaded_error)`; `limit not detected: status error instead of rate_limited`
- **Repair:** Clear a cooldown whose last_error is overloaded_error when svc.anthropic-status is operational (`ashley accounts enable`).
- **By hand:** Update the patterns. Parse rate_limit_event from stream-json instead (see claude_swap_notes).
- **Could break when:** Anthropic changing limit wording or the time zone in messages.
- **Code:** grid/ashleyd.js:57-81,240-246; extension/src/core/protocol.ts:111-150

### `container.claude-remote-control-contract` Remote Control and auth login behaviour

Depends on: `container.image-contract`, `container.claude-version-supported`

- **Assumes:** phone.js depends on this CLI behaviour. `claude auth login` in a pty prints a https://claude.(com|ai)/... URL containing oauth/authorize and reads the code from the pty ending in \r. A wrong code gets 'Invalid code'. `claude auth status` prints JSON with loggedIn. `claude remote-control` prints a session URL containing /code and refuses setup-tokens. The .claude.json keys hasCompletedOnboarding, theme and projects[p].hasTrustDialogAccepted suppress prompts. ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL or CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC turn Remote Control off.
- **Check:** grid (in the worker, slow): run docs/research/rc_probe.sh. Pass = a sign-in URL matching URL_RE with oauth/authorize within 20 s, auth status JSON with a loggedIn key, and the expected 'must be logged in' text from remote-control without a login.
- **Looks like:** `phase stays 'login' (no URL matched)`; `three sign-in attempts did not work`; `phase error: unreadable auth status`
- **By hand:** Update URL_RE, pretrust keys and prompt handling in phone.js. Phone access stays off meanwhile.
- **Could break when:** A new OAuth domain, device-code flow, changed onboarding keys, or Remote Control flags or subscription rules.
- **Code:** grid/phone.js:29-30,66-189; docs/research/rc_probe.sh

## Lab folder (23)

### `lab-folder.access` Lab-folder access state (as seen from the laptop)

Depends on: `grid-login.ashley-cli`

- **Assumes:** `ashley access whoami --json` reports admin, granted or a request state. Workers use the shared container only after access is granted.
- **Check:** Laptop: `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid '~/.ashley/bin/ashley access whoami --json'` exits 0 with JSON that has granted true or admin true (a pending state is reported, not failed).
- **Looks like:** `unknown command (old grid scripts)`; `denied: <message>`; `it comes with lab access`
- **By hand:** File the request from the wizard's last step; the lead approves it in 'Ashley: Lab access'.
- **Could break when:** Changes to the access mechanism on the grid.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:68-71; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:561-586

### `lab-folder.volume-mounted` CephFS lab volume mounted with free quota

Depends on: `account.oschome-group`

- **Assumes:** /rs/rs_grp_oschome (CephFS, 20 TB, group oschome) is mounted on the login node and on the compute nodes, and has space left under its quota.
- **Check:** login node: `stat -f -c %T /rs/rs_grp_oschome` prints ceph, and `df-ceph oschome` shows usage < 90%. Optionally `getfattr -n ceph.quota.max_bytes /rs/rs_grp_oschome`.
- **Looks like:** `No such file or directory: /rs/rs_grp_oschome`; `Disk quota exceeded (EDQUOT) during npm install or publish`; `Transport endpoint is not connected`
- **By hand:** The lead frees space or asks C&IT for more.
- **Could break when:** The allocation expires or needs renewal, or the path changes at a storage migration.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:43, 51-53, 448

### `lab-folder.admin-identity` Lab admin = owner of /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared

Depends on: `lab-folder.volume-mounted`

- **Assumes:** The lab admin is whoever owns /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared (`stat %U`, currently go2432). ASHLEY_ADMIN is consulted only when stat fails.
- **Check:** login node: `stat -c %U /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared` prints go2432, and `ashley access whoami --json` shows lab_admin go2432.
- **Looks like:** `only the owner of /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared can run access init`; `only the lab admin (<x>) can manage requests`; `only the lab admin publishes containers`
- **By hand:** Ask C&IT to restore ownership of shared/ to the lab admin.
- **Could break when:** A storage migration that chowns the tree to root or a service account leaves the lab with no admin.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:451-453, 73, 537, 602

### `lab-folder.acl-support` setfacl present; CephFS honours POSIX ACLs

Depends on: `lab-folder.volume-mounted`

- **Assumes:** setfacl and getfacl exist on the login node, and CephFS honours POSIX ACLs (confirmed 2026-09-28 for the admin). Panasas homes do not, which is why access state lives on the volume.
- **Check:** login node (admin): `f=$(mktemp -p /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/access/requests); setfacl -m u:$USER:r-- $f && getfacl -p $f | grep -q user:$USER:r--; rm -f $f`.
- **Looks like:** `setfacl: <path>: Operation not supported`; `setfacl: command not found`; `<id> approved, but N part(s) of the lab folder could not be opened (not owned by you?)`
- **By hand:** Ticket to hpc@wayne.edu: the CephFS mount has lost ACL support.
- **Could break when:** CephFS remounted without ACLs, or a migration to a filesystem with NFSv4-only ACLs.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:467, 472, 475-497; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:50-52

### `lab-folder.permission-layout` Volume/shared/access modes as `access init` sets them

Depends on: `lab-folder.admin-identity`, `lab-folder.acl-support`

- **Assumes:** After `ashley access init`: the volume root is o-rwx with default ACL o::---. shared/ is 2750 with a default ACL of u::rwx,g::r-x,o::---. Everything under shared/ is group read-only. access/ and access/status are 2710, access/requests is 2700, and each status/<id>.json is 600 plus u:<id>:r--. The old world-writable inbox is gone, and `library publish` keeps shared/ashley at 2750. ARCHITECTURE.md lags the code: it still describes the 1733 drop-box and 2775.
- **Check:** login node: `stat -c '%a %n' /rs/rs_grp_oschome /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/access /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/access/status /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/access/requests`. Pass = root other bits 0, shared 2750, ashley 2750, access 2710, status 2710, requests 2700. Also `test ! -e /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/access/inbox`.
- **Looks like:** `approved students cannot traverse (state approved_unreadable)`; `grid users outside the lab can read shared/ (silent)`; `access/inbox still present`
- **Repair:** Admin: `ashley access init`. It is idempotent and re-applies approved users.
- **By hand:** The lead runs `ashley access init`.
- **Could break when:** C&IT resets modes on the volume, or a member creates a world-readable directory at the root.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:430-447, 536-558, 79; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:145-193

### `lab-folder.member-dir-private` Each member's /rs/rs_grp_oschome/<id> closed to others

Depends on: `lab-folder.permission-layout`

- **Assumes:** The volume root is traversable by approved users, so /rs/rs_grp_oschome/$USER (the member's own tree, bound into workers) must be o-rwx. cmd_adopt closes it; cmd_install does not.
- **Check:** login node: `stat -c %a /rs/rs_grp_oschome/$USER` ends in 0, and `getfacl -p /rs/rs_grp_oschome | grep -q '^default:other::---'`.
- **Looks like:** `other grid users can list /rs/rs_grp_oschome/<id>/ashley/workspaces (silent)`
- **Repair:** `[ -O /rs/rs_grp_oschome/$USER ] && chmod o-rwx /rs/rs_grp_oschome/$USER`. This is safe because only the owner acts on their own directory.
- **By hand:** `chmod o-rwx /rs/rs_grp_oschome/<id>`.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:96, 539-545; docs/research/access_review_2026-09-28.json

### `lab-folder.acl-grant` Approved users hold read ACLs on the whole lab folder

Depends on: `lab-folder.acl-support`, `lab-folder.permission-layout`

- **Assumes:** Approve sets --x on the volume root, r-x plus a default r-X on shared/, and a recursive r-X plus default ACLs on every child except access/ and symlinks. It fails on files the admin does not own. It walks every inode, which can outlast the extension's 120 s ssh timeout, and the ssh is then killed mid-walk, possibly leaving partial grants. Content added later is covered by the default ACLs only when created in place; cp -a and mv keep the old ACLs.
- **Check:** login node (admin), per approved user: `getfacl -p /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley/library.json | grep -q 'user:<id>:r'`, and `find /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared -path '*/access' -prune -o ! -user $USER -print | head` is empty.
- **Looks like:** `<id> approved, but N part(s) of the lab folder could not be opened (not owned by you?)`; `cannot read the shared container <sandbox> (ask your lead for the oschome group)`; `extension access action times out at 120 s`
- **Repair:** Admin: `ashley access admin resync`, run as a Slurm job rather than over a 120 s ssh call.
- **By hand:** The lead runs resync. Files owned by other members must be chowned or re-created by the admin.
- **Could break when:** As shared/ grows (datasets), the walk time grows with it.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:475-497, 611-617, 626-627; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:83-86

### `lab-folder.status-files` Per-applicant status file readable only by that applicant

Depends on: `lab-folder.permission-layout`, `lab-folder.acl-support`

- **Assumes:** Each applicant has status/<id>.json (600 plus u:<id>:r--) and --x path ACLs on the volume root, shared/, access/ and status/, so student_state can read its own decision. Ingest writes a status file only for users that `getent passwd` resolves through SSSD; others are skipped silently.
- **Check:** login node (student): `test -r /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/access/status/$USER.json`, and the state in `ashley access status --json` agrees with it.
- **Looks like:** `state stuck at filed after the admin decided`; `approved_unreadable`; `an applicant never appears in the admin list (getent failed)`
- **Repair:** Admin: run `ashley access admin list` (it ingests) or `ashley access init` (it re-applies path_traverse).
- **By hand:** The lead re-runs the admin list.
- **Could break when:** An SSSD outage or identity-source change on the login node.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:449-474, 508-532

### `lab-folder.access-requirements` Grid-side eligibility: tokens verify and bashrc block present

Depends on: `grid-login.verify-claude-token`, `grid-login.verify-hf-token`, `grid-login.verify-github-token`, `grid-login.bashrc-block`

- **Assumes:** `ashley access requirements --json` passes only when CLAUDE_TOKEN, HF_TOKEN and GITHUB_TOKEN are ok and the bashrc block is present. The wizard also requires every earlier step done and the Claude Code and Remote-SSH extensions installed.
- **Check:** login node: `ashley access requirements --json` prints {"ok":true,"missing":[]}.
- **Looks like:** `not eligible yet:`; `finish the setup first; requests are for working environments`; `the grid did not answer the requirements check`
- **Repair:** Resolve the sub-node named in the missing[] list.
- **By hand:** Fix the named wizard step and run the wizard again.
- **Could break when:** Inherits the risks of the Anthropic OAuth check.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:569-586; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:588-609

### `lab-folder.access-state-machine` Lab access progresses none > filed > pending > approved

Depends on: `grid-slurm.held-request-job`, `grid-slurm.squeue-privatedata`, `lab-folder.status-files`, `lab-folder.acl-grant`, `lab-folder.access-requirements`

- **Assumes:** Lab access moves none -> filed (held job) -> pending (admin ingest opened a status file) -> approved, denied or removed. Approved means library.json is readable, and reset lets the student ask again. In lab mode (ASHLEY_LAB=1, the default) students never build their own container and wait at this node.
- **Check:** student: `ashley access whoami --json` state. Admin: `ashley access admin list --json`. Consistency rules: approved implies library.json is readable; filed implies a held ashley_access job exists.
- **Looks like:** `Waiting for lab access (for days)`; `your request was already decided (denied); ask your lead to reset it if you need to ask again`; `no container yet: it comes with lab access (the last setup step; your lead approves it)`; `approved_unreadable`
- **Repair:** approved_unreadable: the admin runs resync. filed with no held job: refile.
- **By hand:** Remind the lead. The admin page drafts the oschome-group email to hpc@wayne.edu.
- **Could break when:** This step depends on a human: approvals wait on the lead's response time, and the flow stalls if the admin leaves the lab.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:131-135, 258-259, 453, 508-520, 559-600

### `lab-folder.library-json` library.json parseable by the python-free grep parser

Depends on: `lab-folder.volume-mounted`

- **Assumes:** library.json is written by json.dump(indent=1), with one flat object per entry and string fields. The grid CLI parses it with grep and sed (the login node has no python), and that parse breaks on nested objects, escaped quotes or a '}' inside a string.
- **Check:** login node: `ashley library` lists a default, and `ashley library --json | tail -1` parses on the laptop.
- **Looks like:** `no shared library readable at /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley (ask your lead for the oschome group)`; `no library entry 'X' (ashley library)`; `adopt dies with an empty sandbox field`
- **By hand:** The admin re-publishes the entry and never hand-edits library.json with nested values.
- **Could break when:** A future schema field with a nested object breaks lib_field.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:51-71; grid/bootstrap.sbatch:102-117

### `lab-folder.shared-container-readable` Default library entry's sandbox and claude readable by the user

Depends on: `lab-folder.library-json`, `lab-folder.acl-grant`, `grid-node.runtime-path`

- **Assumes:** For this user, through group bits or ACLs, the default entry's sandbox/usr/local/bin/node is executable and its claude_bin is readable.
- **Check:** login node: `s=$(grep -o '"sandbox": *"[^"]*"' /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley/library.json | head -1 | cut -d'"' -f4); test -x $s/usr/local/bin/node`, then run `ashley adopt`.
- **Looks like:** `cannot read the shared container <sandbox> (ask your lead for the oschome group)`; `cannot read <claude_bin>`
- **Repair:** After the admin's resync, run `ashley adopt <default>`.
- **By hand:** Ask the lead to approve or resync.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:85-103

### `lab-folder.library-publish` An admin publish merges the entry and leaves the tree group-readable

Depends on: `lab-folder.admin-identity`, `grid-slurm.partition-secp-secondary`, `grid-slurm.export-environment`, `container.sandbox-build`, `grid-slurm.bootstrap-job`

- **Assumes:** An image can be published only if it provides /usr/local/bin/node (Node 22), npm and python3; the library.json merge runs the container's python3. docker://nvidia/cuda:*-runtime images provide neither node nor python3, so the INSTALL.md CUDA example stops at the build check. Found by reading, not run.
- **Check:** Login node: grep -c '"<name>"' /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley/library.json -> 1; stat -c %a /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley -> 2750; the publish log has 'library entry written: <name>'.
- **Looks like:** `build with <rt> failed:`; `STOP: sandbox built but no runtime could exec it`; `python3: not found`; `only the lab admin publishes containers`; `ENOENT at npm install on the shared path (seen once, fixed by binding a shared ROOT)`; `LIBRARY=1 needs ROOT_OVERRIDE`; `npm error code ENOENT (shared ROOT not bound; fixed in a38812e)`; `'published' without 'library entry written'`
- **Repair:** Rerun the publish (the merge is idempotent).
- **By hand:** Publish from a base with Node 22 (for example a node:22-bookworm image with CUDA libraries added), not from bare nvidia/cuda.
- **Could break when:** Node 22 reaches end of life in April 2027 and the node:22-bookworm tag freezes. Debian bookworm reaches end of life after that.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:72-81; grid/bootstrap.sbatch:62-73, 99-122; docs/INSTALL.md:88-95

### `lab-folder.library-entry` library.json is readable and its default entry points at real files

Depends on: `lab-folder.access-approved`, `grid-login.ashley-scripts-installed`

- **Assumes:** /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley/library.json is readable by the student, parses with the grep-based parser (one-level objects, no '}' inside strings), names a default, and the entry's sandbox, claude_bin and runtime exist and are readable/executable.
- **Check:** Login node: ashley library --json -> available true; for the default: test -x <sandbox>/usr/local/bin/node && test -r <claude_bin> && test -x <runtime>.
- **Looks like:** `no shared library readable at /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley (ask your lead for the oschome group)`; `no library entry '<name>' (ashley library)`; `cannot read the shared container <path> (ask your lead for the oschome group)`; `cannot read <claude_bin>`
- **Repair:** ashley adopt <default> once the entry is valid.
- **By hand:** Lab admin: republish the entry or run ashley access admin resync.
- **Could break when:** A library.json schema change or a 'purpose' text containing '}' breaks lib_field; entries pinned to an old runtime path break at OS upgrade.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:51-61,85-103; grid/bootstrap.sbatch:99-121

### `lab-folder.acl-on-compute-nodes` Named-user ACLs on the lab folder are enforced for a non-owner on compute nodes

Depends on: `lab-folder.library-entry`, `grid-slurm.node-probe`

- **Assumes:** Approval grants read through setfacl u:<id>:r-X; the only test was as the owner (whose access never consults the named entry), and compute nodes may mount CephFS with different ACL options than the login node. Not known yet.
- **Check:** Compute node, as a student: PROBE(test -x /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley/containers/<entry>/usr/local/bin/node && echo READABLE).
- **Looks like:** `REFUSING: sandbox <path> incomplete (on the node while the login node reads it)`; `Permission denied`
- **Repair:** Admin: ashley access admin resync.
- **By hand:** Run the non-owner test from the access review (a second account, ls on login and via srun on a node); report mount differences to C&IT.
- **Could break when:** CephFS client changes (kernel vs fuse, client_acl_type) at OS upgrades.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:475-487,626-627; grid/worker.sbatch.tpl:33; docs/research/access_review_2026-09-28.json (grid/ashley:449 finding)

### `lab.cephfs-mounted` Lab CephFS volume mounted everywhere

Depends on: `grid.scripts`

- **Assumes:** /rs/rs_grp_oschome is mounted on the login node and every compute node, and the allocation (made 2026-09-21) is live.
- **Check:** grid: `stat -f -c %T /rs/rs_grp_oschome` reports ceph. The same holds inside the worker allocation.
- **Looks like:** `no shared library readable at /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared/ashley`; `Transport endpoint is not connected`; `No such file or directory`
- **By hand:** Lead checks the allocation with `df-ceph oschome` and contacts hpc@wayne.edu.
- **Could break when:** Allocation term ending, path renamed (/rs to another prefix), mount method change.

### `lab.volume-quota` Lab volume not full

Depends on: `lab.cephfs-mounted`

- **Assumes:** The 20 TB oschome volume has room. Workspaces, npm caches, containers and every member's data share it, and CephFS quotas are per directory.
- **Check:** grid: `df-ceph oschome`, or `getfattr -n ceph.dir.rbytes` vs `ceph.quota.max_bytes` on /rs/rs_grp_oschome. Warn at 90 %, fail at 98 %.
- **Looks like:** `Disk quota exceeded`; `EDQUOT`
- **By hand:** Lead frees space or requests more.
- **Could break when:** Dataset growth.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:42-44; grid/bootstrap.sbatch:36-43

### `lab.posix-acl-effective` POSIX ACLs actually work on CephFS

Depends on: `grid.acl-tools`, `lab.cephfs-mounted`

- **Assumes:** setfacl on the lab volume takes effect. The mount has ACL support (kernel client acl option, or client_acl_type=posix_acl). grid.acl-tools only checks that the binaries exist; acl_revoke and path_traverse discard errors.
- **Check:** grid (admin, or a student in their own dir): create a temp file under /rs/rs_grp_oschome/$USER, `setfacl -m u:$USER:r--` it, confirm with `getfacl`, then delete it.
- **Looks like:** `setfacl: Operation not supported`; `approved, but N part(s) of the lab folder could not be opened`; `approved_unreadable`
- **By hand:** Ask hpc@wayne.edu to mount with ACL support. Meanwhile, have C&IT add students to the oschome group.
- **Could break when:** A CephFS remount or client change without ACLs.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:486-521

### `lab.library-json-parseable` library.json readable by the bash parser

Depends on: `lab.cephfs-mounted`

- **Assumes:** lib_field, lib_default and lib_names parse library.json with grep and sed. That works only while every entry is a flat object (no nested {}) with string fields, in json.dump(indent=1) form.
- **Check:** grid: for each name in lib_names, lib_field sandbox and claude_bin are non-empty and match the python3 json parse inside the container.
- **Looks like:** `no library entry '<name>'`; `adopted with an empty sandbox`
- **By hand:** Keep entries flat, or parse with python3 in the container.
- **Could break when:** Adding a nested field (env, gpu details) to an entry.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:54-61; grid/bootstrap.sbatch:102-117

### `lab.library-runtime-path-current` Library runtime path still exists

Depends on: `lab.library-json-parseable`, `grid.module-system`

- **Assumes:** Each entry's runtime is an absolute module path baked in at publish time (/wsu/el7/gnu7/apptainer/1.2.2/bin/apptainer). adopt copies it into every student's RUNTIME, so a stale path comes back after each adopt even when grid.runtime repaired it.
- **Check:** grid: each entry's runtime is executable.
- **Looks like:** `REFUSING: runtime ... missing on <node>`; `configured runtime ... is gone`
- **Repair:** Admin: rewrite each entry's runtime to the discover_runtime path (python3 in the container, .part+rename), then `ashley access admin resync`.
- **By hand:** Lead republishes the containers.
- **Could break when:** An OS upgrade from el7 to el8/el9 changes the /wsu/el7 prefix, and a module is retired.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:90,100; grid/bootstrap.sbatch:111

### `lab.library-readable-by-students` Every container file is readable

Depends on: `lab.posix-acl-effective`

- **Assumes:** After publish (chmod -R g+rX, then resync ACLs), no file in a sandbox or npm prefix stays unreadable to approved students. Image files with mode 0600 or 0700 would otherwise fail at exec.
- **Check:** grid (admin, slow): `find <sandbox> <npm prefix> ! -perm -g+r -print -quit` finds nothing.
- **Looks like:** `Permission denied inside the container for an approved student`; `EACCES: node_modules/...`
- **Repair:** Admin: `chmod -R g+rX` on the tree, then `ashley access admin resync`.
- **By hand:** Lead runs the repair.
- **Could break when:** New images with restrictive modes.
- **Code:** grid/bootstrap.sbatch:118-119; grid/ashley:499-511

### `lab.admin-identity` Lab admin is the owner of shared/

Depends on: `lab.cephfs-mounted`, `grid.identity-lookup`

- **Assumes:** The lab admin is whoever owns /rs/rs_grp_oschome/shared (stat -c %U). Every admin and student decision depends on it.
- **Check:** grid: the owner of shared/ exists in passwd and equals the expected lead (for example ASHLEY_ADMIN or the recorded admin).
- **Looks like:** `only the lab admin (<unknown>) can manage requests`; `student sees 'you are the lab admin'`
- **By hand:** Ask hpc@wayne.edu to restore ownership of shared/ to the lead.
- **Could break when:** C&IT chowning the volume, or the lead leaving.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:475-477

### `lab.pending-requests-age` Access requests are not waiting unseen

Depends on: `grid.lab-folder`

- **Assumes:** Requests are ingested only when the admin runs an admin command (for example opening Ashley). Nothing runs on a schedule.
- **Check:** grid (admin): status files in state pending with 'requested' older than 3 days. Warn when any exist.
- **Looks like:** `student stuck at 'Waiting for lab access'`
- **By hand:** Lead opens Ashley: Lab access and decides.
- **Could break when:** Lead turnover.

## Outside services (21)

### `external.github-api` GitHub API contract (GET /user)

Depends on: `network.https-egress`

- **Assumes:** GET https://api.github.com/user with 'Authorization: Bearer <token>' and a User-Agent returns 200 JSON containing 'login'; 401 means a bad token. The laptop sends User-Agent 'ashley-wsu'. The grid extracts the login with grep '"login": *"..."'.
- **Check:** From the laptop or the login node, without a token: `curl -s -m 10 -H 'User-Agent: ashley-diag' https://api.github.com/user` returns HTTP 401 with 'Requires authentication'. That shows the endpoint is alive and unchanged. Any 5xx is an outage (see external.vendor-status).
- **Looks like:** `an unauthenticated status other than 401`; `an HTML body instead of JSON`; `HTTP 401 Bad credentials`; `HTTP 403 Request forbidden by administrative rules. Please make sure your request has a User-Agent header`; `HTTP 403 API rate limit exceeded`
- **By hand:** Nothing for students. If the contract changed, update the validate entry in services.ts and cmd_verify_tokens in grid/ashley.
- **Could break when:** API versioning (X-GitHub-Api-Version) or changes to the response shape; token prefix changes (the paste pattern accepts only ghp_ and github_pat_).
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:38-42; extension/src/core/wizard.ts:361-382; grid/ashley:797-801

### `external.huggingface-api` Hugging Face API contract (whoami-v2)

Depends on: `network.https-egress`

- **Assumes:** GET https://huggingface.co/api/whoami-v2 with 'Bearer hf_...' returns 200 compact JSON whose first 'name' field is the user. The grid extracts it with grep '"name":"[^"]*"', which allows no space after the colon.
- **Check:** From the laptop or the login node, without a token: `curl -s -m 10 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://huggingface.co/api/whoami-v2` returns 401.
- **Looks like:** `404 (endpoint renamed)`; `an unauthenticated status other than 401`; `HTTP 401 Invalid credentials in Authorization header`; `HTTP 429 (whoami is rate-limited)`; `HF_TOKEN rejected or unreachable (on the grid, when the JSON is pretty-printed or its fields reordered)`
- **By hand:** If the response shape changed, update the grep in cmd_verify_tokens (grid/ashley), or parse the JSON with python3 or node.
- **Could break when:** Changes to formatting or field order; stricter rate limits on whoami; the token page ignoring its query parameters.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:48-56; grid/ashley:793-797

### `external.anthropic-oauth-probe` Anthropic /v1/models accepts subscription tokens for the check

Depends on: `network.https-egress`

API keys use X-Api-Key. This is the only automated proof that a subscription token works, on the laptop and on the grid (verify-tokens).
- **Check:** Laptop: an unauthenticated GET https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models returns 401 with JSON error type authentication_error.
- **Looks like:** `403 for a valid OAuth token (Ashley would call it rejected)`; `400 invalid beta flag`
- **By hand:** Update the validate headers in services.ts and grid verify-tokens together, then rebuild.
- **Could break when:** Anthropic retiring the oauth beta flag, restricting OAuth tokens to Claude Code's own endpoints, or bumping anthropic-version.

### `external.npm-registry` npm can fetch the pinned @anthropic-ai/claude-code from the registry

Depends on: `network.https-egress`, `container.dns-tls-egress`

- **Assumes:** registry.npmjs.org is reachable from the container, version 2.1.283 is still published with its linux-x64 native binary, and the user's ~/.npmrc (visible because $HOME is bound) does not set another registry, ignore-scripts=true or omit=optional, any of which can leave a broken install. npm_config_cache points at $ROOT/npm-cache.
- **Check:** Compute node: CEXEC(bash -c 'npm view @anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.283 version; npm config get registry; npm config get ignore-scripts') -> 2.1.283, https://registry.npmjs.org/, false.
- **Looks like:** `npm ERR! code ENOTFOUND`; `npm ERR! 404 Not Found`; `npm error code ETARGET`; `npm error code E404`; `npm error code ENOTFOUND`; `npm error network request to https://registry.npmjs.org/... failed`; `npm error code EINTEGRITY`; `STOP: npm install failed`
- **Repair:** Remove $ROOT/npm-cache (or npm cache verify) and rerun the bootstrap with UPDATE=1; set npm_config_registry=https://registry.npmjs.org/ explicitly in the install command.
- **By hand:** Fix ~/.npmrc; if the version was deprecated, choose a newer pin and republish.
- **Could break when:** Anthropic could steer distribution away from npm toward the native installer; npm policy changes; registry outages.
- **Code:** extension/package-lock.json; extension/package.json:383-399

### `external.anthropic-api` Anthropic API contract (GET /v1/models)

- **Assumes:** GET https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models returns 200 with 'data' for a valid credential: either an API key in X-Api-Key with anthropic-version 2023-06-01, or a subscription OAuth token as Bearer with 'anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20'. An invalid credential returns 401. A 200 on the OAuth path has not been observed with a real setup-token (see account.claude-oauth-token).
- **Check:** From the laptop or the login node, without a credential: `curl -s -m 10 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models` returns 401, with a JSON error of type authentication_error.
- **Looks like:** `HTTP 401 authentication_error`; `HTTP 403 permission error`; `HTTP 400 invalid beta header`; `HTTP 429 or 529 overloaded`
- **By hand:** If the beta header or the endpoint changed, update services.ts:66 and grid/ashley:807 together.
- **Could break when:** High. The oauth-2025-04-20 beta header could be retired, or OAuth tokens restricted to Claude Code's own traffic; anthropic-version could be bumped.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/services.ts:66,80; grid/ashley:803-813

### `external.claude-remote-control` Phone access through Claude Remote Control on the worker

Depends on: `network.compute-node-egress`, `account.claude-subscription`

- **Assumes:** Remote Control accepts only a full claude.ai login; setup-tokens and API keys are refused. So phone.js keeps a separate login in ~/.ashley/remote/config, obtained by running `claude auth login` in a pty (util-linux `script` inside the container) with the paste-code flow: the laptop opens the link, the student copies the code, and `ashley phone code` delivers it on stdin. `claude remote-control` then runs in a pre-trusted folder with CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC unset, using outbound HTTPS only. The login lasts about 30 days.
- **Check:** On the laptop, `ssh wsu-grid "~/.ashley/bin/ashley phone status --worker default --json"` shows job_running true, status.phase 'running' and a session_url. On the compute node, `command -v script` succeeds inside the container (the diag node.probe requires 'script-ok').
- **Looks like:** `three sign-in attempts did not work; turn phone access off and on to try again`; `Claude did not accept that code: copy the whole code from the page (it is long) and send it again`; `the phone sign-in expired; sign in again`; `Remote Control stopped (exit N); restarting in N s`; `Phone access did not start: no answer from the worker`
- **Repair:** Run `ashley phone off`, then `on`, to restart the flow (safe). The worker already restarts Remote Control with backoff.
- **By hand:** Run Ashley: Phone access again, sign in with the Claude account made with the WSU e-mail, and copy the whole code.
- **Could break when:** Remote Control's prompts, the trust-dialog key (hasTrustDialogAccepted), URL formats (claude.ai versus claude.com), plan requirements or the paste-code login flow changing; `script` missing from a new container image.
- **Code:** grid/phone.js:1-16,52-60,77-100,103-160; grid/ashley:640-666,781-788; extension/src/extension.ts:531-576; extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:88-102; docs/RESEARCH.md:120,142

### `external.github-push-via-grid` Pushing the Ashley repository to GitHub through the grid

Depends on: `laptop.git`, `grid-login.sftp-subsystem`, `grid-login.git-https-github`

- **Assumes:** tools/push_via_grid.sh is the only way to push: the laptop checkout has no git remote configured. The script runs `git bundle create --all`, copies the bundle to ~/ashley.bundle with scp, then runs `ssh -o BatchMode=yes wsu-grid 'bash -lc ...'`. On the grid it clones the bundle into ~/ashley-repo, checks out main, and pushes main and the tags to https://github.com/GregorySchwing/ashley.git. The token is GITHUB_TOKEN from the LOGIN environment, that is Greg's shell startup files, not ~/.ashley/secrets.env. The remote command ends with `| tail -3; rm -f ~/ashley.bundle` and has no pipefail, so the script exits 0 even when the push fails.
- **Check:** Before: on the laptop, `git rev-parse --verify main`; on the login node, `bash -lc 'test -n "$GITHUB_TOKEN" && echo set'` prints set. After, outside the script: on the login node, `git ls-remote https://github.com/GregorySchwing/ashley.git refs/heads/main` (with the same credential helper) must equal the laptop's `git rev-parse main`.
- **Looks like:** `no GITHUB_TOKEN in the grid login environment`; `remote: Permission to GregorySchwing/ashley.git denied to <login>.`; `fatal: Authentication failed for 'https://github.com/GregorySchwing/ashley.git/'`; `error: src refspec main does not match any`; `remote: Repository not found. (after a move to OpenSpineConsortium)`; `the script prints three lines of git output and exits 0 although nothing was pushed`
- **Repair:** When the login environment lacks GITHUB_TOKEN, source ~/.ashley/secrets.env inside the same remote shell; it is the user's own mode-600 file and is never printed. Always finish with the ls-remote comparison and exit non-zero on a mismatch.
- **By hand:** When the repository moves, pass its URL as $1. Export a repo-scoped token in the grid login environment, or run the GitHub token step. If Windows sockets are flaky, run the script from WSL, which needs its own ssh key and config because the WSL mirror was removed.
- **Could break when:** Fine-grained token expiry and organisation approval ('pending') once the repository moves to OpenSpineConsortium, where Greg's grid token is not a member; the old git on the login node.
- **Code:** tools/push_via_grid.sh:1-15; docs/CHECKPOINT_2026-09-28.md:151-157

### `external.vendor-status` Service status of GitHub, Hugging Face and Anthropic

- **Assumes:** A 5xx, a 429 or a network error from a vendor means 'cannot verify now', not 'bad token'. The laptop treats it that way (network:true). The grid's `access requirements`, however, blocks on anything other than 'ok', so an outage blocks lab-access requests.
- **Check:** On the laptop: status.indicator is 'none' in https://www.githubstatus.com/api/v2/status.json and in Anthropic's Statuspage (https://status.anthropic.com/api/v2/status.json), and the Hugging Face status page is reachable. Informational only: report WARN at most.
- **Looks like:** `HTTP 500, 502 or 503 from validateToken`; `HTTP 529 overloaded_error`; `CLAUDE_TOKEN present (the check answered HTTP 5xx)`
- **Repair:** Retry with backoff. Safe.
- **By hand:** Wait for the outage to end, and do not renew tokens during it.
- **Could break when:** The status-page URLs moving.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/wizard.ts:373-381; grid/ashley:575-590,803-813; grid/ashleyd.js:57

### `external.grid-maintenance` Grid maintenance or outage

- **Assumes:** When the login node refuses connections or times out from every network, including the VPN, while the rest of the internet works, or when Slurm answers 'Unable to contact slurm controller', the cause is a C&IT maintenance window or an outage, not the student's setup. Queued requests survive, because they are files.
- **Check:** On the laptop: TCP 22 to warrior fails AND https://www.wayne.edu answers AND, on the VPN, OnDemand also fails; together these mean maintenance is likely. When the login node is reachable: `scontrol ping` prints 'Slurmctld(primary) at ... is UP'.
- **Looks like:** `slurm_load_jobs error: Unable to contact slurm controller (connect failure)`; `sbatch: error: Batch job submission failed: Unable to contact slurm controller (connect failure)`; `ssh: connect to host warrior.grid.wayne.edu port 22: Connection refused`
- **By hand:** Wait, and check C&IT's announcements or write to hpc@wayne.edu. Nothing on the laptop needs to change.
- **Could break when:** Major maintenance windows are when the OS, sshd, host keys, module names and paths change together, so run the full troubleshooter afterwards.
- **Code:** extension/src/core/gridApi.ts:55-58; grid/ashley:32-37,700-704

### `external.docker-hub` Docker Hub serves docker://node:22-bookworm to the grid

Depends on: `grid-node.egress`

- **Assumes:** registry-1.docker.io, auth.docker.io and its CDN are reachable, the tag exists, and the anonymous pull quota for the grid's shared egress IP is not exhausted. Only builds (admin publishes, --build-own) pull; students adopt.
- **Check:** Compute node: PROBE(curl -s -m 8 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://registry-1.docker.io/v2/) -> 401 (reachable, auth required). Rate headroom: fetch an anonymous token from auth.docker.io for repository:ratelimitpreview/test:pull and HEAD its manifest; read the ratelimit-remaining header (> 0).
- **Looks like:** `toomanyrequests: You have reached your pull rate limit`; `dial tcp: lookup registry-1.docker.io: no such host`; `manifest unknown`; `FATAL: While making image from oci registry: error fetching image to cache`
- **Repair:** Retry after the rate window, or rebuild from the same official image through a mirror: IMAGE=docker://public.ecr.aws/docker/library/node:22-bookworm.
- **By hand:** Authenticate pulls (APPTAINER_DOCKER_USERNAME/PASSWORD) or wait.
- **Could break when:** Docker Hub has tightened anonymous limits before; all grid nodes likely share few egress IPs, so one heavy user exhausts the quota for everyone.
- **Code:** grid/bootstrap.sbatch:33,69; grid/ashley:75

### `external.anthropic-api-from-node` api.anthropic.com answers from compute nodes

Depends on: `container.dns-tls-egress`

- **Assumes:** The worker's claude reaches api.anthropic.com; the token check (GET /v1/models with anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20 and anthropic-version: 2023-06-01) returns 200 for a valid setup-token token and 401 for an invalid one. The grid's verify treats any other code (000, 403, 429, 5xx) as 'present', which is not verification.
- **Check:** Compute node: CEXEC(curl -s -m 15 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models) -> 401 unauthenticated (reachable). Login node: ashley verify-tokens -> 'CLAUDE_TOKEN ok'.
- **Looks like:** `CLAUDE_TOKEN present (the check answered HTTP 000)`; `overloaded_error`; `API Error: 529`; `net=000`
- **Repair:** Retry with backoff; report 000 as unverified rather than passing.
- **By hand:** Check status.anthropic.com; report persistent 000 from nodes to hpc@wayne.edu.
- **Could break when:** The oauth beta header or API version is retired; the endpoint's behaviour for OAuth tokens changes (the code a valid token receives was not yet recorded with a real token).
- **Code:** grid/ashley:500-507,647-658; extension/src/core/services.ts:59-71; docs/research/access_review_2026-09-28.json (grid/ashley:597 finding)

### `svc.anthropic-oauth-models-contract` Anthropic token check semantics

Depends on: `network.internet`

- **Assumes:** GET https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models with Bearer <setup-token>, anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20 and anthropic-version 2023-06-01 answers 200 for a valid subscription token and 401 for an invalid one. If the beta header is retired or OAuth is refused on /v1/models, every valid token looks 'rejected' and lab access is blocked.
- **Check:** laptop or grid: send a request with a deliberately invalid token. Pass = HTTP 401 with error.type authentication_error. Any 400 or 404 mentioning oauth or beta means the contract changed.
- **Looks like:** `CLAUDE_TOKEN rejected for a token that works in claude -p`; `HTTP 400 OAuth authentication is currently not supported`
- **By hand:** Verify the token by a 1-turn claude -p on the worker instead, and update the check.
- **Could break when:** Anthropic changing the oauth beta header or endpoint policy.
- **Code:** grid/ashley:836-847; extension/src/core/services.ts:66

### `svc.anthropic-status` Anthropic service health

Depends on: `network.internet`

- **Assumes:** Failures are attributed to Ashley only when Anthropic itself is up. An outage (529 overloaded_error) otherwise looks like a limit or a broken worker.
- **Check:** laptop: GET https://status.claude.com/api/v2/status.json. The indicator is 'none' (warn on minor, major or critical).
- **Looks like:** `overloaded_error`; `API Error: 529`; `api_error`
- **By hand:** Wait for the incident to clear.
- **Could break when:** Status page URL changes.
- **Code:** grid/ashleyd.js:57

### `svc.hf-whoami-contract` Hugging Face whoami JSON as grepped

Depends on: `grid.internet`

- **Assumes:** The grid parses https://huggingface.co/api/whoami-v2 with grep '"name":"[^"]*"' (no space after the colon). A reformatted response would make valid tokens look rejected and block lab access.
- **Check:** grid: whoami-v2 with the stored token. The grep yields a name iff python3 or node JSON parsing yields one.
- **Looks like:** `HF_TOKEN rejected or unreachable for a valid token`; `Hugging Face token missing or rejected`
- **By hand:** Use a whitespace-tolerant pattern ('"name": *"'), as the GitHub check already does.
- **Could break when:** HF API response formatting or path deprecation (whoami-v2).
- **Code:** grid/ashley:829-830

### `svc.dockerhub-pull` Base image pull from Docker Hub

Depends on: `node.egress-endpoints`

- **Assumes:** `apptainer build --sandbox` can pull docker://node:22-bookworm anonymously from compute nodes. Many users share the grid's outbound IP, which counts toward Docker Hub's pull limits.
- **Check:** grid (in the worker): GET https://registry-1.docker.io/v2/ answers 401 (reachable). A token fetch for library/node reports ratelimit-remaining > 0. Run before a publish or bootstrap.

- **Could break when:** Docker Hub policy changes. node:22 end of life (2027-04-30); Debian bookworm end of life.
